+\epsilon\int_{u}^{u_{0}}\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{u}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\sin\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{1+\epsilon}}\left[|r\Omega^{2}\alpha(u,\bar{v},\theta^{A})|^{2}+|\psi(u,\bar{v},\theta^{A})|^{2}\right]\\
\lesssim\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\sin\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,\left[|r\Omega^{2}\alpha(u_{0},\bar{v},\theta^{A})|^{2}+|\psi(u_{0},\bar{v},\theta^{A})|^{2}\right]\\
+E_{u_{0}}[\Psi](v_{1},v_{2})+\underline{E}_{v_{2}}[\Psi](-\infty,u_{0}).

(6.34)

The above estimate remains valid with
\mathring{\not{\nabla}}r\Omega^{2}\alpha
,
\mathring{\not{\nabla}}\psi
on both sides of the estimates
without requiring a higher order energy for
\Psi
. The plan now is to use (
6.34
) to show that the following quantity vanishes: Define
\widetilde{\Psi}
via

\displaystyle\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v}\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v}\widetilde{\Psi}=r\Omega^{2}\alpha,

(6.35)

with
\widetilde{\Psi}
,
\not{\nabla}_{v}\widetilde{\Psi}
vanishing at
v_{1}
. It is clear that

\displaystyle\mathrm{RW}[\widetilde{\Psi}]|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=0.

(6.36)

Note that

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{v}\mathrm{RW}[\widetilde{\Psi}]=\psi+\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}(5-3\Omega^{2}-\mathring{\not{\Delta}})\not{\nabla}_{v}\widetilde{\Psi}+6M\widetilde{\Psi}.

Since
\not{\nabla}_{u}(r\Omega^{2}\alpha)=0
at
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
, we also have

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{v}\mathrm{RW}[\widetilde{\Psi}]|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=0.

Therefore, we have by (
3.52
) from Lemma
3.1
that
\widetilde{\Psi}
satisfies the Regge–Wheeler equation (
RW
).

Now, by definition, we have

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{v}\widetilde{\Psi}=\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}(u,v)\int_{v_{1}}^{v}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}r(u,\bar{v})^{3}\alpha(u,\bar{v},\theta^{A}).

(6.37)

We make use of the fact that
r\alpha
decays pointwise towards
\mathcal{I}^{-}
to estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sin\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,\left|\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}\int_{v_{1}}^{v}r^{3}\alpha\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\right|^{2}&=\frac{\Omega^{4}}{r^{4}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sin\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,\left[\int_{v_{1}}^{v}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\,\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{u_{0}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{u}\,\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}\psi\right]^{2}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sin\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,\left[\int_{v_{1}}^{v}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\,\int_{-\infty}^{u_{0}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{u}\,\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}|\psi|\right]^{2}\\
&\lesssim\frac{(v_{2}-v_{1})}{r(u,v_{1})}\int_{v_{1}}^{v}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\,\int_{-\infty}^{u_{0}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{u}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sin\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}|\psi|^{2}\\
&\lesssim\frac{(v_{2}-v_{1})}{r(u,v_{1})}\times\Big(\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\sin\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,\left[|r\Omega^{2}\alpha|_{u_{0}}|^{2}+|\psi|_{u_{0}}|^{2}\right]\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+E_{u_{0}}[\Psi](v_{1},v_{2})+\underline{E}_{v_{2}}[\Psi](-\infty,u_{0})\Big),\end{split}

(6.38)

where we used (
6.34
) in the last step.
Thus
\|\not{\nabla}_{v}\widetilde{\Psi}(u,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}([v_{1},v_{2}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}\to 0
as
u\to-\infty
. Combining this with
\widetilde{\Psi}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=0
, Proposition
6.1
implies
\widetilde{\Psi}=0
. Thus
\alpha=0
.
∎

The argument above allows us to conclude the following result, which will later on allow us to conclude uniqueness for the full system (
3.9
)–(
3.32
).

Corollary 6.2
.

Let
\alpha
be a solution to (
Teuk
) on
(u,v,\theta^{A})\in(-\infty,u_{0}]\times[v_{1},v_{2}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
such that
\underline{E}_{v_{2}}[\Psi](-\infty,u_{0})<\infty
, where
\Psi=\left(r^{2}\Omega^{-2}\not{\nabla}_{u}\right)^{2}r\Omega^{2}\alpha
. If
\alpha
satisfies

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\frac{1}{r(u,v_{1})}\int_{v_{1}}^{v}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\,r(u,\bar{v})^{2}\alpha(u,\bar{v},\theta^{A})=0,\qquad\not{\nabla}_{u}(r\Omega^{2}\alpha)|_{\mathcal{C}}=0,

(6.39)

then
\alpha=0
.

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{\Psi}
be as in the proof of Proposition
6.4
. We compute
E_{u}[\widetilde{\Psi}](v_{1},v_{2})
by directly estimating

\|\not{\nabla}_{v}\widetilde{\Psi}(u,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}([v_{1},v_{2}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2}\lesssim\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sin\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\left(\int_{v_{1}}^{v}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}r^{3}\alpha\right)^{2}\\
\lesssim(v_{2}-v_{1})^{2}\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\sin\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\theta\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,\,(r\alpha)^{2}\lesssim(v_{2}-v_{1})^{2}\|r\alpha(u,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}([v_{1},v_{2}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2},

(6.40)

and by similarly estimating

\displaystyle\|r^{-1}\widetilde{\Psi}(u,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}([v_{1},v_{2}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(v_{2}-v_{1})^{\frac{3}{2}}}{r(u,v_{1})}\|r\alpha(u,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}([v_{1},v_{2}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2})},

(6.41)

\displaystyle\|r^{-1}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}\widetilde{\Psi}(u,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}([v_{1},v_{2}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{(v_{2}-v_{1})^{\frac{3}{2}}}{r(u,v_{1})}\|\mathring{\not{\nabla}}r\alpha(u,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}([v_{1},v_{2}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2})},

(6.42)

Combining (
6.34
) with (
6.41
), we see that
\|r^{-1}\widetilde{\Psi}(u,\cdot,\cdot)\|_{L^{2}([v_{1},v_{2}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}
decays to 0 as
u\to-\infty
.
Thus,
r^{-1}\Psi
decays in measure as
u\to-\infty
by Vitali’s convergence theorem. Furthermore, the assumption that
\Psi
is of finite energy at
v_{2}
means the same applies to
\widetilde{\Psi}
by combining (
6.34
) with (
6.40
), (
6.41
), (
6.42
).
A computation shows

\displaystyle\frac{1}{r(u,v_{1})}\int_{v_{1}}^{v}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\,r(u,\bar{v})^{2}\alpha(u,\bar{v},\theta^{A})=\left[\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v}\widetilde{\Psi}+\frac{1}{r}\widetilde{\Psi}\right]\Bigg|_{(u,v,\theta^{A})},

(6.43)

hence
\not{\nabla}_{v}\widetilde{\Psi}
, too, decays in measure as
u\to-\infty
. By Corollary
6.1
, we then deduce that
\widetilde{\Psi}=0
. It follows that
\alpha=0
.
∎

7
Construction of the (unique) scattering solution to (
3.9
)–(
3.32
)

In this section, we present the full proof of Theorem
5.1
. First, we prove uniqueness of scattering solutions in §
7.1
.
We then present the construction of the
\ell\geq 2
-part of the scattering solution in §
7.2
and of the
\ell<2
-part in §
7.3
. The latter will be essentially trivial, all the difficulty is contained in
\ell\geq 2
.

7.1
Uniqueness of solutions to the scattering problem

We now prove the uniqueness clause of Theorem
5.1
:

Proposition 7.1
.

Assume that
\mathfrak{S}
is a solution to (
3.9
)–(
3.32
) realising a vanishing seed scattering data set
\mathfrak{D}
. Assume moreover that

\displaystyle\underline{E}_{v}[\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}](-\infty,u_{0})<\infty

(7.1)

for all
v\geq v_{1}
. Then
\mathfrak{S}
vanishes identically.

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{S}
be a scattering solution realising
\mathfrak{D}
.
Since
\mathfrak{D}
is trivial, we deduce from Prop.
5.1
that, in particular, the restriction to
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
of
{\mathrm{tr}{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{g}}}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
,
K
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
belonging to
\mathfrak{S}
vanishes as well.
By (
3.32
), we infer that
\not{\nabla}_{u}(r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}})
vanishes along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
as well. The condition (
7.1
), together with

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int_{v_{1}}^{v}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\,r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}(u,\bar{v},\theta^{A})

(7.2)

\displaystyle=-

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\left(\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\left(\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}(u,v,\theta^{A})-\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}(u,v_{1},\theta^{A})\right)\right)=0

(7.3)

implies by Corollary
6.2
that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}\equiv 0.
It follows that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}\equiv 0
.

Consecutively integrating (
3.17b
), (
3.31b
) as well as (
3.30b
) from
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
then yields that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}\equiv 0\equiv\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
as well as
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}\equiv 0
. It follows from (
3.38
) that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}\equiv 0
, and thus
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}\equiv 0
(cf. Cor.
5.2
).

With
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
both vanishing identically, we now apply Proposition
4.7
to deduce that the
\ell\geq 2
component of
\mathfrak{S}
is pure gauge, i.e.
\mathfrak{S}_{\ell\geq 2}=\mathfrak{S}_{f_{\ell\geq 2}}+\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{f}_{\ell\geq 2}}+\mathfrak{S}_{(q_{1},q_{2})_{\ell\geq 2}}
for some functions
f_{\ell\geq 2}
,
\underline{f}_{\ell\geq 2}
and
(q_{1},q_{2})_{\ell\geq 2}
.

We now show that these functions vanish:
Firstly, the vanishing of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
forces
\underline{f}_{\ell\geq 2}=0
. (Recall that the kernel of
\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}
is spanned by the
\ell=0,1
-modes.)
Secondly, the vanishing of
\lim_{u\to-\infty}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}
implies that the outgoing gauge solution must have
\partial_{v}f_{\ell\geq 2}=0
, and the vanishing of, say,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
, implies that
f_{\ell\geq 2}=0
.
Thirdly, the vanishing of
\lim_{u\to-\infty}r^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{b}
implies that
\partial_{v}{q_{1}}_{\ell\geq 2}
,
\partial_{v}{q_{2}}_{\ell\geq 2}
vanish, and the fact that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\hat{\not{g}}}
is trivial on
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
leads to
{q_{1}}_{\ell\geq 2}={q_{2}}_{\ell\geq 2}=0
.

At this point, we know that
\mathfrak{S}
is supported only on
\ell=0,1
, so it is a linear combination of a pure gauge solution, a linearised nearby Schwarzschild solution, and a linearised Kerr solution by Prop.
4.6
.

From the fact that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=0
, we can infer that the linearised Kerr solution must vanish.

In order to also show that the linearised nearby Schwarzschild solution vanishes, we assume that it doesn’t:
Then this solution generates nonvanishing
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}=-\frac{2M\mathfrak{m}}{r^{3}}=-\frac{2}{r}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{K}_{\mathfrak{m}}
along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
, supported on
\ell=0
, by Prop.
4.1
.
But since
\mathfrak{S}
has
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=0=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{K}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=0
, it must now be possible to find pure gauge solutions supported on
\ell=0
to kill off
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{K}_{\mathfrak{m}}
along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
.
An inspection of the expressions given in Propositions
4.3
–
4.4
shows that such solutions do not exist:
Since the outgoing gauge solution decays too fast near
\mathcal{I}^{-}
(
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}_{f}\sim r^{-4}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{K}\sim r^{-3}
), the only candidate to kill off the leading order behaviour of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}_{\mathfrak{m}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{K}_{\mathfrak{m}}
is the ingoing gauge solution, but this has
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}_{\underline{f}}=-\tfrac{3}{r}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{K}_{\underline{f}}
.
Thus the linearised nearby Schwarzschild solution also vanishes.

We now know that
\mathfrak{S}
is a pure gauge solution supported on
\ell=0,1
, i.e.
\mathfrak{S}=\mathfrak{S}_{f_{\ell\leq 1}}+\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{f}_{\ell\leq 1}}+\mathfrak{S}_{(q_{1},q_{2})_{\ell\leq 1}}
.
The condition that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=0
implies that
\underline{f}_{\ell\leq 1}(u,\theta^{A})=f_{\ell\leq 1}(v=v_{1},\theta^{A})
, so
\underline{f}_{\ell\leq 1}
is independent of
u
.
The condition that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}\to 0
as
u\to-\infty
then implies that
\partial_{v}f_{\ell\leq 1}=0
, so we have that
\underline{f}_{\ell\leq 1}(u,\theta^{A})=\tilde{f}_{\ell\leq 1}(\theta^{A})=f_{\ell\leq 1}(v,\theta^{A})
for some
\tilde{f}
.
Comparing now the expressions for
{\mathrm{tr}{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{g}}}}
generated by each of these solutions along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
, we deduce that
\tilde{f}_{\ell=1}=0
. (The expression for
{\mathrm{tr}{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{g}}}}
generated by
(q_{1},q_{2})
comes with a different
r
-weight.)
We thus have that
\underline{f}_{\ell\leq 1}(u,\theta^{A})=C=f_{\ell\leq 1}(v,\theta^{A})
for some constant
C
, and hence, in view of Remark
4.1
,
\mathfrak{S}_{f_{\ell\leq 1}}+\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{f}_{\ell\leq 1}}
=0.

Thus, we have that
\mathfrak{S}=\mathfrak{S}_{(q_{1},q_{2})_{\ell\leq 1}}
.
Since
r^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{b}\to 0
as
u\to-\infty
, and since
{\mathrm{tr}{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{g}}}}
vanishes along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
, it finally follows that
\mathfrak{S}=0
.
∎

7.2
Construction of the
\ell\geq 2
-part of the solution to the scattering problem

We now present an explicit construction of a scattering solution
\mathfrak{S}
to (
3.9
)–(
3.32
) realising a given smooth seed scattering data set
\mathfrak{D}
such that
\mathfrak{S}
and
\mathfrak{D}
are related via Definition
5.2
.

Proposition 7.2
.

Given a smooth seed scattering data set
\mathfrak{D}_{\ell\geq 2}
supported on
\ell\geq 2
and satisfying the assumptions of Theorem
5.1
, there exists a scattering solution
\mathfrak{S}_{\ell\geq 2}
realising
\mathfrak{D}_{\ell\geq 2}
. By the previous Prop.
7.1
, this is the unique scattering solution realising
\mathfrak{D}_{\ell\geq 2}
.

Since the proof of Prop.
7.2
is quite long, we first give a detailed overview.

7.2.1
Overview of the construction

The construction is organised into the following steps:

(I)

Out of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
and data on
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
(from Prop.
5.1
), we define various tensor fields such as
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
and
{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{P}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
. (These are to be thought of as the pointwise limits of
r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
,
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
and
\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
of the eventual solution.)

(II)

We now construct the unique scattering solution
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
to (
RW
) such that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
restricts to
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\bm{\Uppsi}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
and
\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
tends to
{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{P}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
via Theorem
6.1
, where
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\bm{\Uppsi}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
has been constructed in Cor.
5.2
.
Using (
6.26
) then gives us the bound
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}\lesssim r^{1/2}

(III)

We then define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
by integrating
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}=(\tfrac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{u})^{2}(r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}})
(cf. (
3.37
)) twice from
\mathcal{I}^{-}
, defining the boundary terms at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
by using the definitions of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
from step
(II)
and demanding (
3.32
) to hold at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
. We then show that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
satisfies (
Teuk
) by virtue of Lemma
3.1
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
satisfying (
RW
).

(IV)

Having constructed
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
in the previous step, we now define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
by integrating (
3.17b
) and (
3.31b
) from
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
, respectively, with the boundary terms
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
defined in Prop.
5.1
.
The fact that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
satisfies (
Teuk
) implies that (
3.32
) is satisfied.
We then show that
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
converges to
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
as
u\to-\infty
, and similarly for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
.

(V)

We define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
by integrating (
3.30b
) from
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
, with boundary term
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upsigma}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
as in Prop.
5.1
.
We then prove that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
satisfies the scalar Regge–Wheeler equation (
RW-scalar
). This is done as follows:

•

We show that the
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,
of (
3.22
), replacing
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\eta}}
with
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
, is satisfied:

\displaystyle\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,r^{2}\Omega^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}=r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}-\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\,r^{3}\Omega^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}.

(7.4)

This is proved by showing that
\not{\nabla}_{v}\eqref{eq:construct:sigmatrick1}
is satisfied using (
3.17b
), (
3.31b
) and (
3.30b
) and using that (
7.4
) holds along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
by Prop.
5.1
.

•

We then show that the
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,
of (
3.28b
) is satisfied:

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{u}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}=-\mathring{\not{\Delta}}r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}-6M\Omega^{2}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}.

(7.5)

This again follows by virtue of (
7.5
) being satisfied along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
and by showing that
\not{\nabla}_{v}\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\eqref{eq:construct:sigmatrick2}
is satisfied using (
3.31b
), (
3.30b
), (
3.17b
) as well as (
7.4
).

Equations (
3.30b
) and (
7.5
) together imply that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
solves (
RW-scalar
).

(VI)

Next, we define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}+4\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}(0,r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}})
. Then
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}
satisfies the Regge–Wheeler equation (
RW
). From here one, we can to some extent mirror the previous approach, with a few extra difficulties that we will highlight below:
We define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
by integrating
(\tfrac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{2}(r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}})
twice from
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
with data terms coming from Prop.
5.1
. The fact that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}
satisfies (
RW
) is then used to show that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
satisfies (
Teuk
) via Lemma
3.1
.

(VII)

We would like to define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
by integrating (
3.18a
) from
\mathcal{I}^{-}
. Now, in general, we will have decay no better than
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}=\mathcal{O}(|u|^{-\frac{5}{2}-})
(cf. (
5.10
)), (
3.18a
), so
\not{\nabla}_{u}(\Omega^{-1}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}})=-r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
will not be integrable.
This problem is resolved by commuting (
3.18a
) with
\not{\nabla}_{v}
, using that
\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
decays one power faster than
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
: We define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
by integrating

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{u}\not{\nabla}_{v}\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}=-\left(2-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\right)r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}-\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v}(r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}})

(7.6)

first in
u
from
\mathcal{I}^{-}
and then in
v
from
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
.
We can then define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}
via (
3.27
) and deduce that (
3.28a
) holds by virtue of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
satisfying (
Teuk
).

(VIII)

The difficulty at this point is that we have no way of directly verifying (
3.30a
) (
\partial_{u}(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}})=-\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}
). The work-around to this problem is to define a different
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
that satisfies (
3.30a
), and to then show that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
as follows:

•

Define
r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
as the solution to
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\eqref{eq:lin:divxhb}
(cf. (
7.4
)):

\displaystyle r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}:=\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}-\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\frac{r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}}{\Omega}.

(7.7)

We directly deduce from the definition that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
satisfies (
3.30a
).

•

As in step
(V)
, we can then prove that
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\eqref{eq:lin:beb4}
holds with
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
replaced by
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
, from which we can infer that
r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
satisfies (
RW-scalar
).
We then show that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
attain the same data on
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}\cup\mathcal{I}^{-}_{v\geq v_{1}}
and appeal to the uniqueness clause of Theorem
6.1
to deduce
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
.

(IX)

We define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\eta}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\eta}}
via (
3.17a
) and (
3.18b
), respectively.
It is then a simple computation to confirm that (
3.19b
) and (
3.20a
) hold as well.
Then, using, in particular, equations (
3.30a
) and (
3.30b
), we deduce (
3.24
).
Since thus
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\eta}}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\eta}})=0
, we can define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}
as solution to (
3.12
).
From
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}
, we define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\omega}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\omega}}
via (
3.11
), and (
3.20b
), (
3.19a
) immediately follow from (
3.20a
), (
3.19b
).

(X)

Similarly, since we have already proved that the
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,
-parts of (
3.23
) and (
3.22
) vanish in (
7.4
), (
7.7
), we can define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\left(\Omega\mathrm{tr}\chi\right)}
via (
3.22
) and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\left(\Omega\mathrm{tr}\underline{\chi}\right)}
via (
3.23
).
Directly from this definition, we infer (
3.16
) by appropriately differentiating (
3.22
) in
v
and (
3.23
) in
u
.

(XI)

As the penultimate step, we define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}
and prove that all remaining equations of (
3.9
)–(
3.32
) featuring
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}
are satisfied (except the Gauss equation, since we have not yet defined the metric components
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\hat{\not{g}}}
and
{\mathrm{tr}{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{g}}}}
).
The easiest way to do this is as follows:

•

Prove that
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\eqref{eq:lin:al33}
is satisfied. This allows to define
\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho},0)
, and thus
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}
, via (
3.34
), i.e. as solution to

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}=2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho},-r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}})+6M(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}-r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}).

(7.8)

•

By acting with
\not{\nabla}_{u}
or
\not{\nabla}_{v}
on (
7.8
) and using all the previous equations, we can deduce that (
3.29a
), (
3.29b
) are satisfied. We can then deduce the remaining Bianchi equations (
3.31a
), (
3.28b
).

•

We deduce (
3.14
) by multiplying (
3.22
) by
r\Omega
and then acting with
\not{\nabla}_{u}
. We similarly prove (
3.15
).

•

We finally prove (
3.21
) by acting on the definition (
3.12
) of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}
with
\not{\nabla}_{u}\not{\nabla}_{v}
and using the already established equations.

(XII)

We define the remaining metric coefficients
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{b}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\hat{\not{g}}}
and
{\mathrm{tr}{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{g}}}}
via integration of (
3.13
) from
\mathcal{I}^{-}
with
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
as data, and integration of (
3.10b
) and (
3.9b
) from
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
with
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\hat{\not{\mathtt{g}}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
and
\mathrm{tr}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{\mathtt{g}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
(defined in Prop.
5.1
) as data.
By further taking the
\not{\nabla}_{v}
-derivative of (
3.9a
) and (
3.10a
), we can establish that (
3.9b
) and (
3.10b
) hold everywhere since they hold along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
.
The final thing left to do is to prove the Gauss equation (
3.25
). For this, we prove that
\not{\nabla}_{v}(r^{2}\eqref{eq:lin:K})
holds and that, by construction, (
3.25
) holds along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
.
We have now constructed a solution to (
3.9
)–(
3.32
). By construction and Prop.
5.1
, this solution realises the prescribed seed data.
This completes the proof.

7.2.2
The full details of the construction

Proof of Proposition
7.2
.

We now present the full details of the construction. The reader already convinced by the overview should feel free to skip this section.

Step
(I)
: Defining scattering data at
\mathcal{I}^{-}

We first define a number of fields at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
that will play the role of data at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
in the construction carried out in the subsequent sections:

Definition 7.1
.

Given a smooth seed scattering data set
\mathfrak{D}
, we define the following fields at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}-\mathring{\not{\nabla}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{K}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}},

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=-\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}},

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\bm{\uppsi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=-2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}},

(7.9)

where
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{K}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}}
has been defined in (
5.6
),
as well as

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{S}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=-4\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}\mathtt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}},

\displaystyle{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{P}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=-2(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}-4)\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}-6M\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}.

(7.10)

Remark 7.1
.

The above definitions will be used as data at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
for
r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
,
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}
,
\not{\nabla}_{v}(r^{2}\Omega^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}})
, and
\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
, respectively.

The definition above will be used explicitly to define the scattering solution over the next few pages.
On the other hand, we will now define a number of fields at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
for which it will later follow that they are attained as limits of the constructed solution.

Definition 7.2
.

Given a smooth seed scattering data set
\mathfrak{D}
, we define the following fields at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\hat{\not{\mathtt{g}}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}(v)=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\hat{\not{\mathtt{g}}}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}}+\int_{v_{1}}^{v}2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}(\bar{v})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v},

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{\mathtt{g}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}(v)=\mathrm{tr}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{\mathtt{g}}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}}-\int_{v_{1}}^{v}\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{b}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}(\bar{v})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v},

(7.11)

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\upomega}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=\partial_{v}\mathtt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}},

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=2\mathring{\not{\nabla}}\mathtt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}},

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{K}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{K}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}},

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\left(\mathtt{\Omega}\mathrm{tr}\upchi\right)}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=2\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{K}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}+4\mathtt{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}.

Remark 7.2
.

We will show in our construction that the fields above are attained as limits of the quantities
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\hat{\not{g}}}
,
{\mathrm{tr}{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{g}}}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\omega}
,
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\eta}}
,
r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{K}
and
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\left(\Omega\mathrm{tr}\chi\right)}
.
On the other hand, it will follow from our construction that the limits of the following quantities vanish as
u\to-\infty
:
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\omega}}
,
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\left(\Omega\mathrm{tr}\underline{\chi}\right)}
,
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\eta}}
,
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
,
r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}
,
r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
,
r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}
,
r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
.

Step
(II)
: Constructing
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
using scattering theory for (
RW
)

We now construct
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
via the scattering theory for the Regge–Wheeler equation (
RW
) given by Theorem
6.1
.

Proposition 7.3
.

For a smooth seed scattering data
\mathfrak{D}
, there exists a unique smooth finite energy solution
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
to (
RW
) such that, for any
v\geq v_{1}
:

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\|\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}-{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{P}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}\|_{L^{2}([v_{1},v]\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}=0,\qquad\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\bm{\Uppsi}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}},

(7.12)

where
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\bm{\Uppsi}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
is defined in Cor.
5.2
and
{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{P}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
is defined in Def.
7.1
.
Moreover, we have the estimate

|\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}|\leq C_{n}(v)r^{1/2},\qquad\forall n\in\mathbb{N}

(7.13)

for some
C_{n}(v)
depending continuously on
v
, and for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1}
, we have

\lim_{u\to-\infty}\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}=\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m-1}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{P}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}

(7.14)

as
u\to-\infty
, uniformly on
[v_{1},v]
for any
v\geq v_{1}
.

Proof.

By the decay rate in Cor.
5.2
, we deduce that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\bm{\Uppsi}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
has finite energy along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
. The result thus follows from the scattering theory for (
RW
) (Thm.
6.1
) as well as (
6.26
) and (
6.27
).
∎

Step
(III)
: Constructing
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}

Next, we construct
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
by integrating (
3.34
) from
\mathcal{I}^{-}
:

Definition 7.3
.

For a smooth seed scattering data
\mathfrak{D}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
arising via Proposition
7.3
, define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}
by integrating

\displaystyle\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}=\not{\nabla}_{u}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}

(7.15)

in
u
from
\mathcal{I}^{-}
with data
-2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\bm{\uppsi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
.
Similarly, we define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
by integrating

\displaystyle\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}=\not{\nabla}_{u}r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}

(7.16)

with data
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
(defined in Def.
7.1
). By construction,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
.

Note that these definitions are well-defined in view of estimate (
7.13
).

We now show that
\not{\nabla}_{v},\mathring{\not{\nabla}}
-derivatives of
r^{3}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}
converge to
\not{\nabla}_{v},\mathring{\not{\nabla}}
-derivatives of
-2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
as
u\to-\infty
, and a similar statement for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
:

Lemma 7.1
.

For a smooth seed scattering data
\mathfrak{D}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\psi}}},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
arising via Definition
7.3
, we have, for any
n,m\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}(u,v,\theta^{A})\to\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m}\left(-2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}\right)(v,\theta^{A}),

(7.17)

\displaystyle\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m}r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}(u,v,\theta^{A})\to-\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m+1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}(v,\theta^{A})

(7.18)

as
u\to-\infty
. For any
v\geq v_{1}
, this convergence is uniform on
[v_{1},v]
.

Proof.

We compute that

\displaystyle\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{u}\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}=\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v}\not{\nabla}_{u}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}=\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}-\frac{3\Omega^{2}-1}{r}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}.

(7.19)

Integrating in
u
and using (
7.13
) as well as (
7.14
), we see that
\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}
converges to a limit as
u\to-\infty
and that this convergence is uniform on
[v_{1},v]
for any
v\geq v_{1}
.
Since
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}\to-\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
, it follows that
\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}\to-\not{\nabla}_{v}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
.
The result for
\not{\nabla}_{v}
- and
\mathring{\not{\nabla}}
-derivatives follows by straight-forward commutation.

The result for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
follows analogously.
∎

Next, we want to show that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
solves the Teukolsky equation (
Teuk
). For this, we first show:

Lemma 7.2
.

For a smooth seed scattering data
\mathfrak{D}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
arising via Definition
7.3
, we have that

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\mathrm{Teuk}^{+2}[r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}]=\lim_{u\to-\infty}\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{u}\mathrm{Teuk}^{+2}[r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}]=0

(7.20)

for any
v\geq v_{1}
, where the operator
\mathrm{Teuk}^{+2}
was defined in Def.
3.2
.

Proof.

We can re-write the operators above as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Teuk}^{+2}[r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}]

\displaystyle=\frac{3\Omega^{2}-1}{r}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}+\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}+\left(-\mathring{\not{\Delta}}+2+\frac{6M}{r}\right)r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}},

(7.21)

\displaystyle\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{u}\mathrm{Teuk}^{+2}[r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}]

\displaystyle=\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}-\left(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}-3\Omega^{2}-1\right)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}+6Mr\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}.

(7.22)

Therefore, using the previous Lemma
7.1
:

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\mathrm{Teuk}^{+2}[r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}]=-2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}-\left(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}-2\right)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}.

(7.23)

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{u}\mathrm{Teuk}^{+2}[r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}]={\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{P}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}+2\left(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}-4\right)\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}-6M\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}.

(7.24)

The right hand sides above both vanish by construction (cf. Def.
7.1
).
∎

We may now infer:

Corollary 7.1
.

For a smooth seed scattering data
\mathfrak{D}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
defined in Definition
7.3
, we have that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
satisfies the Teukolsky equation (
Teuk
).

Proof.

Using Lemma
3.1
, we have that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{u}\right)^{2}\mathrm{Teuk}^{+2}[r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}]=\mathrm{RW}[\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}]=0

(7.25)

since
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
satisfies the Regge–Wheeler equation (
RW
). We integrate this equation twice from
\mathcal{I}^{-}
, where the boundary terms vanish by Lemma
7.2
.
∎

Step
(IV)
: Constructing of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}

We now construct
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
and show that (
3.32
) is satisfied:

Definition 7.4
.

With
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
defined in Def.
7.3
, define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
to be the unique solutions to (
3.17b
), (
3.31b
) with data
\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
,
\frac{r^{4}}{\Omega}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
respectively.

Lemma 7.3
.

The quantities
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
satisfy eq. (
3.32
)

Proof.

Def.
7.4
, together with the Teukolsky equation (
Teuk
) satisfied by
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
, implies that

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{v}\left[\frac{r^{4}}{\Omega^{4}}\not{\nabla}_{u}r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}+2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\frac{r^{4}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}}{\Omega}-6M\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}\right]=0.

(7.26)

Integrating this in
v
from
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
, and using that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
is related to
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
via (
3.32
) by Proposition
5.1
proves the lemma.
∎

For later purposes, we will also need to verify that
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
and
r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
realise
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
(which we have used to define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\psi}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
) as their limits at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
:

Corollary 7.2
.

For
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
constructed in Definition
7.4
, we have that for any
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{m}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}=\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{m}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}},

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{m}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}=\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{m}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}},

(7.27)

the convergence being uniform in
v
in compact
v
-intervals.

Proof.

We will prove the statement for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
; the proof for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
being similar:
First of all, we have, by definition,

\frac{1}{r}\left(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}(u,v)-\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}(u)\right)=-\frac{1}{r}\int_{v_{1}}^{v}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}(u,\bar{v})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}.

(7.28)

Since
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}(u,v)
converges uniformly to
-\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
on
[v_{1},v]
for any fixed
v\geq v_{1}
by Lemma
7.1
, and since the ratio
\frac{r(u,\bar{v})}{r(u,v)}
tends to 1 uniformly in on
[v_{1},v]
for any fixed
v
, we have that

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{r(u,v)}\int_{v_{1}}^{v}r(u,\bar{v})^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}(u,\bar{v})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}\to-\int_{v_{1}}^{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}(\bar{v})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}(v)-\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}(v_{1})

(7.29)

as
u\to-\infty
for any
v\geq v_{1}
. Using that
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}\to\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}(v=v_{1})
by construction (Prop.
5.1
), it follows that
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}\to\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
as claimed.
The proof for higher derivatives follows from Lemma
7.1
and commuting.
∎

Step
(V)
: Constructing
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}

Definition 7.5
.

Define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
to be the unique solution to (
3.30b
) with data
r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upsigma}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
on
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
.

Corollary 7.3
.

We have for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and some
C_{n}(v)
depending continuously on
v
that

|\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}})|\leq C_{n}(v)r^{\frac{1}{2}}.

(7.30)

In addition, the following convergence is uniform in
v
on any compact
v
-interval:

\lim_{u\to-\infty}\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}=-\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m-1}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}},\qquad\text{for all }m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(7.31)

Proof.

The convergence (
7.31
) follows from the definition of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
and Cor.
7.2
.
The upper bound (
7.30
) follows from the bound (
7.13
), the definition of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
and the rates (
5.10
) for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upsigma}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
.
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

The quantity
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
defined in Definition
7.5
satisfies
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\eqref{eq:lin:divxh}
as well as
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\eqref{eq:lin:be3}
:

\displaystyle\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}=r^{2}\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}-\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}},

(7.32)

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}=-\mathring{\not{\Delta}}\,r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}-6M\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}.

(7.33)

Proof.

Equations (
3.31b
), (
3.30b
) imply

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\,\left(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}-\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{3}\Omega^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}\right)=-\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}=\partial_{v}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}

(7.34)

and, since
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
satisfy (
7.32
) at
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
, equation (
7.32
) propagates for
v\geq v_{1}
.

The proof for (
7.33
) is similar, but involves more computations: We first multiply the LHS of (
7.33
) and differentiate in
v
:

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\,\partial_{u}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}\right)

\displaystyle=\partial_{v}\left(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\right)\partial_{u}\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}+\partial_{u}\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\not{\nabla}_{v}(r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}})

(7.35)

\displaystyle=\partial_{u}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}+\frac{3\Omega^{2}-1}{r}\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}-2(3\Omega^{2}-2)\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}

\displaystyle=\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\left[-2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}+6M\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}\right]-2(3\Omega^{2}-2)\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}},

where we used (
3.31b
) in the second line and (
3.32
) in the third line.
We re-write the angular operator (recall the notation
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,=\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}
) using eq. (
2.30
):

\displaystyle\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{1}\left[-2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\right]=\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{1}\left[\mathring{\not{\Delta}}+1\right]=\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{1}\left[2-\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{1}\right]=\left[2-\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{1}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}\right]\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{1}=\left[\mathring{\not{\Delta}}+2\right]\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{1},

so we have, in particular, that

\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\left(-2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}\right)=\left(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}+2\right)\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}},

and we can write

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\,\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\,\partial_{u}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}\right)=\left(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}+\frac{12M}{r}\right)\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}+6M\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}.

Finally, we apply (
7.32
) to this:

\partial_{v}\left(\,\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\,\partial_{u}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}\right)=\left(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}+\frac{12M}{r}\right)\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}+6M\left(\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}-\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}\right).

(7.36)

Next, we apply the operator
\partial_{v}\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}
to the right hand side of (
7.33
):

\partial_{v}\left(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}+6Mr^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}\right)=-\mathring{\not{\Delta}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}-6Mr\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}-6M\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}.

(7.37)

Combining (
7.36
) and (
7.37
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{u}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}+\mathring{\not{\Delta}}r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}+6Mr^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}\right)=0.

(7.38)

But since (
7.33
) holds along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
, we can now integrate the above from
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
to deduce that it holds everywhere.
∎

Corollary 7.4
.

The quantity
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
constructed in Definition
7.5
satisfies the scalar Regge–Wheeler equation (
RW-scalar
).

Proof.

This follows from (
3.30b
) and (
7.33
).
∎

Step
(VI)
: Constructing
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
.

We are now in position to define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}
and thus
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
:

Definition 7.6
.

Define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}
via

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}:=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}+2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}(0,r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}).

(7.39)

Define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\psi}}}
to be the unique solution to

\displaystyle\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}=\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\psi}}},

(7.40)

with data
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\bm{\uppsi}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\upbeta}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}+6M\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
at
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
.
Define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
to be the unique solution to

\displaystyle\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\psi}}}=\not{\nabla}_{v}(r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}),

(7.41)

with data
r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upalpha}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
at
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
.

Corollary 7.5
.

Since
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
and
\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}(0,r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}})
satisfy the Regge–Wheeler equation (
RW
),
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}
also satisfies (
RW
). Moreover, we have for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and for some
C_{n}(v)
depending continuously on
v
the estimates

\displaystyle|\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}|\leq C_{n}(v)r^{\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle|\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\psi}}}|\leq C_{n}(v)r^{-\frac{1}{2}},

\displaystyle|\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}|\leq C_{n}(v)r^{-5/2},

(7.42)

as well as the uniform convergence on compact
v
-intervals of

\lim_{u\to-\infty}\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}=\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m-1}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\bm{\Uppsi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}-\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}(0,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}})),\qquad\text{for all }m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(7.43)

Proof.

The first of (
7.42
) follows from the estimates (
7.13
), (
7.30
). Then using the definition of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\psi}}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
together with the rates (
5.10
) and (
5.12
), the other two rates follow as well.

The convergence (
7.43
) follows directly from (
7.14
) and (
7.31
).
∎

As we did for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
, we now show that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
satisfies (
Teuk
) by appealing to equation (
3.51
) from Lemma
3.1
.

Corollary 7.6
.

The quantity
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
constructed in Def.
7.6
satisfies the Teukolsky equation (
Teuk
).

Proof.

Since
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}
satisfies the Regge–Wheeler equation (
RW
), we have by Lemma
3.1
that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v}\right)^{2}\mathrm{Teuk}^{-2}[r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}]=0.

(7.44)

A computation analogous to that in the proof of Lemma
7.2
shows that
\mathrm{Teuk}^{-2}[r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}]
,
\not{\nabla}_{v}\mathrm{Teuk}^{-2}[r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}]
vanish at
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
.
∎

Step
(VII)
: Constructing
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}

We now construct
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}
. Note that since
|\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}|\lesssim C(v)r^{-\frac{5}{2}}
, we cannot integrate (
3.18a
) in
u
from
\mathcal{I}^{-}
.
However, since
|\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}|\lesssim C(v)r^{-7/2}
(by (
7.42
)), we can instead integrate the
\not{\nabla}_{v}
-commuted equation:

Definition 7.7
.

Define
\underline{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{S}}}
to be the unique solution to

\not{\nabla}_{u}\underline{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{S}}}=-r^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\psi}}}-\left(2-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\right)r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}},

(7.45)

with data
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{S}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
defined in Def.
7.1
.
Define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
to be the unique solution to

\not{\nabla}_{v}\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}=\underline{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{S}}},

(7.46)

with data
r^{2}\Omega^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
at
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
.

Lemma 7.5
.

The quantity
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
constructed in Definition
7.7
satisfies

\not{\nabla}_{u}\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}=-r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}.

(7.47)

Moreover, we have the following uniform convergence along compact
v
-intervals:

\lim_{u\to-\infty}\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}=\not{\nabla}_{v}^{m-1}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}^{n}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{S}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}},\qquad\text{for all }m\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 1},\,n\in\mathbb{N}.

(7.48)

Proof.

The first statement follows by integrating the identity

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{v}(r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}})=r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\psi}}}+\left(2-\frac{1}{\Omega^{2}}\right)r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}=-\not{\nabla}_{u}\underline{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{S}}}=-\not{\nabla}_{v}\not{\nabla}_{u}\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}

(7.49)

in
v
from
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
, using that (
7.47
) is satisfied along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
by
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upalpha}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
as a consequence of Prop.
5.1
.

The proof of (
7.48
) proceeds exactly as the proof of Lemma
7.1
, using the rates (
7.42
).
∎

Definition 7.8
.

Define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}
to be the unique solution to (
3.27
), with
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}

and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}

. By construction and Prop.

,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\upbeta}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
.

Corollary 7.7
.

The quantity
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}
constructed in Definition
7.8
satisfies (
3.28a
).

Proof.

This follows by computing
\not{\nabla}_{u}(\tfrac{r^{4}}{\Omega^{4}}\eqref{eq:lin:alb4})
, using (

) for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
as well as the Teukolsky equation (
Teuk
) for

. (Recall that the operator

.)
∎

Step
(VIII)
: Constructing
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
and proving that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}

Recall the difficulties addressed in the overview of Step
(VIII)
in §
7.2.1
: Since we have no direct way of inferring (
3.30b
) at this point, we instead define a different
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
:

Definition 7.9
.

Define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
via the
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,
of (
3.23
):

\displaystyle r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}:=\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,r^{2}\Omega^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}-\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{3}\Omega^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}.

(7.50)

By construction and Prop.
5.1
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
restricts to
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upsigma}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
on
\mathcal{C}
.

Lemma 7.6
.

The quantity
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{u}(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime})=\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}.

(7.51)

Proof.

This follows by taking the
\partial_{u}
-derivative of the LHS of (
7.50
) using the already established equations (
3.18a
), (
3.28a
).
∎

In order to infer that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
, we first show that their radiation fields at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
are identical:

Lemma 7.7
.

For
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\partial_{v}(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime})=-\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=\lim_{u\to-\infty}\partial_{v}(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}).

(7.52)

This convergence is uniform in
v
on compact
v
-intervals.

Proof.

Note that we have already shown this for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
.
For
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
, we first take the
\partial_{v}
-derivative of (
7.50
) and use (
7.48
) to find

\lim_{u\to-\infty}\partial_{v}(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime})=\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{S}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}-\lim_{u\to-\infty}\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\not{\nabla}_{v}(\tfrac{r^{3}}{\Omega}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}).

(7.53)

In order to evaluate the limit on the RHS, we compute, using (
3.27
):

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\,\not{\nabla}_{v}(r^{3}\Omega^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}})=\lim_{u\to-\infty}(\not{\nabla}_{v}(r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\psi}}})-6M\not{\nabla}_{v}(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}})).

(7.54)

But

\not{\nabla}_{v}(r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\psi}}})=r^{-1}\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}+\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\psi}}}

tends to 0 as
u\to-\infty
by (
7.42
) and (
7.43
), and so does
\not{\nabla}_{v}(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}})
by (
7.48
).
It thus follows that
\lim_{u\to-\infty}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\not{\nabla}_{v}r^{3}\Omega^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}=0
.
In order to deduce that
\lim_{u\to-\infty}\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{3}\Omega^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}=0
as well, we simply apply
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}
: To be precise, we use that for any 1-form
\beta
:

2\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\beta=-(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}+2)\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\beta.

(7.55)

It follows that

\lim_{u\to-\infty}-(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}+2)\partial_{v}(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime})=-(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}+2)\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{S}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}-\lim_{u\to-\infty}2\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\not{\nabla}_{v}(\tfrac{r^{3}}{\Omega}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}),

(7.56)

The term on the RHS now vanishes by the same argument as above, and, by the invertibility of
\mathring{\not{\Delta}}+2
on
\ell\geq 2
, a Poincaré inequality and Sobolev embedding:

\lim_{u\to-\infty}\partial_{v}(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime})=\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{S}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}},

(7.57)

where the last two equalities follow from definitions of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{S}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}
in Def.
7.1
(and the fact that
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}f=0=\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\nabla}}f
for any
f\in\Gamma^{\infty}{(\mathbb{S}^{2})}
).
∎

We can now show that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
also satisfies (
RW-scalar
):

Lemma 7.8
.

The quantities
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
satisfy the
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,
of (
3.28a
), i.e.:

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}=-\mathring{\not{\Delta}}r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}-6M\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}.

(7.58)

In particular, in view of Lemma
7.6
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
satisfies (
RW-scalar
).

Proof.

This is similar to the proof of (
7.33
). In the same way in which we proved (
7.38
), we first show that

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\left[\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{v}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}-\mathring{\not{\Delta}}r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}-6Mr^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}\right]=0.

(7.59)

Notice that we cannot integrate this from
\mathcal{I}^{-}
, because the object on which
\partial_{u}
is acting does not converge near
\mathcal{I}^{-}
. We again resolve this problem by considering the
\partial_{v}
-derivative:

\displaystyle\partial_{u}\partial_{v}\left[\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{v}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}-\mathring{\not{\Delta}}r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}-6Mr^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}\right]=0.

(7.60)

We integrate this in
u
, using that the boundary term at
\mathcal{I}^{-}
vanishes. Indeed:

Sublemma 1
.

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\partial_{v}\left(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{v}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,(r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}})\right)=\mathring{\not{\Delta}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}=\lim_{u\to-\infty}\mathring{\not{\Delta}}r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}.

(7.61)

Proof of the sublemma.

As in the proof of Lemma
7.7
, we use (
3.27
) to compute

\displaystyle 2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\not{\nabla}_{v}\left(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v}(r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}})\right)=\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}-6M\not{\nabla}_{v}^{2}\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}+6M\frac{(3\Omega^{2}-1)}{r}\not{\nabla}_{v}\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}-12M(3\Omega^{2}-2)\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}};

(7.62)

so we have

\lim_{u\to-\infty}2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\not{\nabla}_{v}\left(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v}(r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}})\right)=\lim_{u\to-\infty}\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}-6M\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{S}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}.

(7.63)

In view of (
7.43
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}={\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\mathtt{P}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}-4\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}(0,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}})

\displaystyle=-2(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}-4)\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}-4\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}(0,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}})-6M\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}

(7.64)

\displaystyle=2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}\left(\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}},-\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}\right)+6M\not{\nabla}_{v}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}},

where we also used (
2.30
).
Acting now on this with
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{u\to-\infty}2\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\not{\nabla}_{v}\left(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v}(r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}})\right)=-(\mathring{\not{\Delta}}+2)(\mathring{\not{\Delta}})\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upbeta}}_{\mathcal{I}^{-}}.

(7.65)

The result then follows using (
7.55
) (and that we are supported on
\ell\geq 2
).
∎

Using this sublemma, we can now integrate (
7.60
) from
\mathcal{I}^{-}
to deduce that

\displaystyle\partial_{v}\left[\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\partial_{v}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}-\mathring{\not{\Delta}}r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}-6Mr^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}\right]=0.

(7.66)

In turn, integrating this from
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
, and verifying that
\partial_{v}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,r^{2}\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}-\mathring{\not{\Delta}}r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}-6M\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}=0
at
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
by computing the
\not{\nabla}_{v}
derivative of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}
in spacetime via (
3.27
), restricting to
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
and using Proposition
5.1
, we conclude the proof of (
7.58
).

Acting now with (
7.58
) on (
7.51
), we infer that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
satisfies (
RW-scalar
).
∎

Corollary 7.8
.

With
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
defined in Def.
7.9
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
defined in Def.
7.5
, we have

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
.

Proof.

Since
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
on
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
and
\lim_{u\to-\infty}\partial_{v}r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}=\lim_{u\to-\infty}\partial_{v}r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
uniformly in
v
, the uniqueness clause of Theorem
6.1
implies
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
by virtue of both
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}^{\prime}
satisfying (
RW-scalar
).
∎

Step
(IX)
: Constructing
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\eta}}
,
