\displaystyle=\alpha^{[-2]}_{\ell}=\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}r_{0}^{-2}\sum_{n=2}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,n,-2}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}+(\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}\log r_{0}+\underline{\mathscr{C}}_{\ell})r_{0}^{-3}\sum_{n=3}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,1,\ell+2,n,-2}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}

(13.6)

\displaystyle+r_{0}^{-3}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell+3}(\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}\underline{R}^{\prime}_{\ell,n}+2M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\underline{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell,n}+\mathcal{O}^{u}(r_{0}^{-\delta}+Mr_{0}^{-1}))\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}

\displaystyle+(-1)^{\ell}M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\frac{\ell(\ell+1)(\ell+2)!}{3(\ell-2)!}r^{-3}(\log r-\log r_{0})

\displaystyle-(-1)^{\ell}M\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}\frac{(\ell-1)(\ell+2)(\ell+2)!}{{8}(\ell-2)!}r^{-4}\log^{2}r+M\mathcal{O}(r^{-4}r_{0}\log r)),

where the constants
\underline{S}_{\ell,p,\ell-s,n,s}
are defined in (
13.15
) and for some constants
\underline{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell,n},\underline{R}^{\prime}_{\ell,n}
, which satisfy, for
n\leq 2
:

\underline{R}^{\prime}_{\ell,n}=-\frac{2(|s|+1)}{\ell(\ell+1)}\underline{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell,n}=(-1)^{\ell}\frac{(\ell-1)!(\ell+2)!}{(\ell+1)!(\ell-2)!}(1+|s|)!\frac{(2-n)!(\ell+n-2)!}{n!(\ell-n+2)!}.

(13.7)

Moreover, the following limits are conserved along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
(cf. (
11.4
), (
11.5
)):

\displaystyle\mathbf{I}^{2-\ell,0}_{\ell}[\alpha^{[-2]}]=\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{2-\ell}\not{\nabla}_{v}\mathrm{A}^{[-2]}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell+1}2M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\frac{(\ell+2)!(\ell+1)}{(\ell-2)!}=\frac{(\ell+2)!}{(\ell-2)!}\overline{\mathbf{I}^{2-\ell,0}_{\ell}[\alpha^{[2]}]},

(13.8)

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{1}{r}(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{4}(\Omega^{2}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}})

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell+1}2M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\frac{\ell(\ell+1)(\ell+2)!}{(\ell-2)!}=\frac{(\ell+2)!}{(\ell-2)!}\overline{\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{4}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{\ell}}.

(13.9)

Finally, the limit
\lim_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}
satisfies:

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upalpha}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}:=\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}=(-1)^{\ell}\left(\frac{12\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}}{\ell(\ell+1)}-4M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\right)r_{0}^{-3}+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-3-\delta}+Mr_{0}^{-4}).

(13.10)

Remark 13.1
.

We only provide the values of
\underline{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell,n}
and
\underline{S}_{\ell,n}^{\prime}
for
n\leq 2
as those will be the only ones that will play a role in later parts of the paper.

13.2
Proof of Theorem
13.1

Compared to the proof of Theorem
11.1
, there are two differences: Firstly, the computation of transversal derivatives along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
is very slightly different, as has already been mentioned in the beginning of this section.

The other, more notable, difference is that we here need to understand more refined asymptotic estimates since we also want to compute the coefficient in front of the
r_{0}^{-3}
-decay of the limit
\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
(cf. (
13.10
)).
Indeed, as can be seen from (
13.6
), the leading order contributions from the first line all vanish at
\mathcal{I}^{+}
, as
\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,0,-2}=\underline{S}_{\ell,1,\ell+2,0,-2}
=0. We will come back to this at the end of the proof.

Proof.

First, we compute the transversal derivatives. In the same way in which we proved Propositions
10.1
and
11.1
, we have:

Proposition 13.1
.

Let
N\geq 2
,
s=-2
. Define the constants

\underline{C}^{\nearrow}_{(N,p)}=\frac{(|s|+p)!}{(N+s+p)!}\prod_{j=0}^{N-1}\tilde{a}^{[s]}_{j,\ell,\ell}=\frac{(|s|+p)!}{(N+s+p)!}(-1)^{N}\frac{(\ell-s)!(N+s+\ell)!}{(\ell-s-N)!(\ell+s)!}.

(13.11)

Then, for some constants
\underline{E}^{\prime}_{n}
that we shall not yet compute,

\left.{(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{N}\alpha^{[s=-2]}}\right|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=\left.\underline{\mathscr{A}}r_{0}^{s-p+N}\underline{C}^{\nearrow}_{(N,p)}\right|_{p=0}+\left.\left(\underline{\mathscr{B}}r_{0}^{s-p+N}\log r_{0}+\underline{\mathscr{C}}r_{0}^{-s-p+N}\right)\underline{C}^{\nearrow}_{(N,p)}\right|_{p=1}\\
+\left.\underline{\mathscr{B}}r_{0}^{s-p+N}(-\delta_{N,0}\cdot(|s|+p)!+\underline{C}^{\nearrow}_{(N,p)}\left(+\sum_{i=p+s+2}^{N+s+p}\frac{1}{i}\right)\right|_{p=1}+M\underline{E}^{\prime}_{n}\underline{\mathscr{A}}r_{0}^{-3+N}+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-3+N-\delta}).

(13.12)

In fact, the formula is valid also for
N=0,1
.

Proof.

For
N\geq 2
, the proof proceeds inductively in the same way as the proof of Proposition
11.1
, starting from (
13.4
). The cases
N=0,1
follow from direct comparison with (
13.3
) and (
13.4
).
∎

Next, we again compute the Minkowskian part of the solution.

Proposition 13.2
.

Let
s=-2
:

\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-s}\frac{1}{i!}\left(\frac{1}{r_{0}}-\frac{1}{r}\right)^{i}\left.{(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{i}\alpha^{[s]}}\right|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=\left.\underline{\mathscr{A}}r_{0}^{s-p}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\cdot(\underline{S}_{\ell,p,\ell-s,n,s}+\mathcal{O}^{u}(Mr_{0}^{-1}+r_{0}^{-1-\delta}))\right|_{p=0}\\
+\left.(\underline{\mathscr{B}}\log r_{0}+\underline{\mathscr{C}})r_{0}^{s-p}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\cdot\underline{S}_{\ell,p,\ell-s,n,s}\right|_{p=1}\\
+\left.\underline{\mathscr{B}}r_{0}^{s-p}\sum_{n=0}^{p-s-1}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\underline{R}_{\ell,p,\ell-s,n,s}\right|_{p=1}\\
+\left.\underline{\mathscr{B}}r_{0}^{s-p}\sum_{n=p-s}^{\ell-s}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\underline{S}_{\ell,p,\ell-s,n,s}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\ell-s-1-n}\frac{1}{\ell-p-k}+\sum_{k=2}^{\ell-s}\frac{1}{k+s+p}\right)\right|_{p=1},

(13.13)

where

\underline{R}_{\ell,p,\ell-s,n,s}:=(-1)^{\ell-p+1}\frac{(\ell-p)!(\ell-s)!(p+|s|)!}{(\ell+p)!(\ell+s)!}\frac{(p-n-s-1)!(\ell+n+s)!}{n!(\ell-n-s)!},

(13.14)

and

\underline{S}_{\ell,p,j,n,s}:=\frac{(-1)^{\ell-s+n}(|s|+p)!(\ell-s)!(2\ell-j+n)!(\ell-p)!}{n!(\ell+s)!(\ell+p)!(\ell-p-j+n)!(j-n)!}.

(13.15)

Remark 13.2
.

Notice, in particular, that

\lim_{v\to\infty}\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-s}\frac{1}{i!}\left(\frac{1}{r_{0}}-\frac{1}{r}\right)^{i}\left.{(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{\ell-s-j+i}\alpha^{[s]}}\right|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=(-1)^{\ell}\frac{12\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}r_{0}^{-3}}{\ell(\ell+1)}+M\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-3}).

(13.16)

This means that we will have to keep track of the
M
-contributions as well in order to obtain an expression for the limit of
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
.

Proof.

We again set
M=0
and argue by linearity.
We have

(r^{2}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{\ell-s}\alpha^{[s=-2]}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=\underline{\mathscr{A}}r_{0}^{\ell-p}\underline{C}^{\nearrow}_{(\ell-s,p,p)}|_{p=0}\\
+r_{0}^{\ell-p}(\underline{\mathscr{B}}\log r_{0}+\underline{\mathscr{C}})\underline{C}^{\nearrow}_{(\ell-s,p,p)}|_{p=1}+\underline{\mathscr{B}}r_{0}^{\ell-p}\underline{C}^{\nearrow}_{(\ell-s,p,p)}\sum_{i=p+s+2}^{N+s+p}\frac{1}{i}|_{p=1}

(13.17)

The result now follows by applying Prop.
14.1
to the
\underline{\mathscr{A}}
and the
\underline{\mathscr{C}}
-terms, and by applying Prop.
14.2
to the
\underline{\mathscr{B}}
-terms. (Cf. the proofs of Propositions
10.8
and
11.3
.)
∎

Finally, we have

Proposition 13.3
.

Let
s=-2
. Then

\alpha^{[s]}_{\ell}=\Omega^{2}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}=\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}r_{0}^{-2}\sum_{n=2}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,n,-2}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}+(\underline{\mathscr{B}}\log r_{0}+\underline{\mathscr{C}})r_{0}^{-3}\sum_{n=3}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,1,\ell+2,n,-2}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\\
+r_{0}^{-3}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell+3}(\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}\underline{R}^{\prime}_{\ell,n}+2M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\underline{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell,n}+\mathcal{O}^{u}(r_{0}^{-\delta}+Mr_{0}^{-1}))\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\\
-\left.M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\underline{C}^{\nearrow}_{(\ell-s,p)}\frac{(\ell-p)(\ell+p+1)!}{(2\ell)!}\frac{(-1)^{p+1-s}}{(p+1-s)!}r^{s-p-1}(\log r-\log r_{0})\right|_{p=0}\\
-\left.M\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}\underline{C}^{\nearrow}_{(\ell-s,p)}\frac{(\ell-p)(\ell+p+1)!}{(2\ell)!}\frac{(-1)^{p+1-s}}{{2}(p+1-s)!}r^{s-p-1}\log^{2}r\right|_{p=1}+M\mathcal{O}(r^{-4}r_{0}\log r).

(13.18)

Proof.

Apart from the
r_{0}^{-3}M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell+3}\underline{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell,n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}
-term, the proof proceeds in the same way as in the previous sections, the only difference being that
C^{\nearrow}_{(\ell-s,p)}
is now replaced by
\underline{C}^{\nearrow}_{(\ell-s,p)}
:
First, we prove that

(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{\ell-s+1}\alpha^{[s]}=\left.-M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\underline{C}^{\nearrow}_{(\ell-s,p)}\frac{(\ell-p)!(\ell+p+1)!}{(2\ell)!}r^{\ell-p}\right|_{p=0}\\
-\left.M\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}\underline{C}^{\nearrow}_{(\ell-s,p)}\frac{(\ell-p)!(\ell+p+1)!}{(2\ell)!}r^{\ell-p}\log r\right|_{p=1}+M\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{\ell})

as in Propositions
10.5
and
11.2
for the
\underline{\mathscr{A}}
and the
\underline{\mathscr{B}}
terms, respectively.
We subsequently compute the nested integrals from Proposition
10.6
as in Propositions
10.7
and
11.4
, and the result follows.

To also construct the
r_{0}^{-3}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell+3}\underline{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell,n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}
-term, with (
13.7
) (which is, in particular, necessary to compute the
M\neq 0
-contribution to the limit (
13.10
)), we need to work a bit harder:

Since we have already captured the contribution coming from the
\underline{\mathscr{B}}
- and the
\underline{\mathscr{C}}
-terms, it now suffices to consider the problem where
\underline{\mathscr{B}}
,
\underline{\mathscr{C}}=0
and
\delta=\infty
. We then do the following Post-Minkowskian type argument:
We split the Teukolsky equation up into equations (
14.3
), (
14.6
) and (
14.5
), where we only pose non-trivial data for (the homogeneous) equation (
14.3
), and pose trivial data for the other two (inhomogeneous) equations.

To be precise, the data we pose for (
14.3
) is that
\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=0
, that
\not{\nabla}_{v}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=0
and that
\lim_{u\to-\infty}(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{2}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}=4\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{1}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}\underline{\mathscr{A}}
(cf. (
13.1
), (
13.3
), (
13.4
)), together with the no incoming radiation condition (
13.2
).
The solution is then given by

\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-s}\frac{1}{i!}\left(\frac{1}{r_{0}}-\frac{1}{r}\right)^{i}(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{i}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
, and is determined by Proposition
14.1
, the result being the first line of (
13.13
); in particular, the coefficients
S_{n}
in (
14.8
) are given by (
13.15
). In particular, we read off that
\lim_{\mathcal{I}^{+}}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}=0
.

We now insert
\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}
as an
inhomogeneity
into (
14.4
).
We can easily see from Proposition
10.5
, eq. (
10.36
) that the solution
\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}
must thus be of the form assumed in Proposition
14.4
, see also Remark
10.5
.
Applying the result of Proposition
14.4
then gives us a handy expression for the corresponding solution
\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}
, namely (
14.22
), with
S_{\ell-s}
given by
\underline{S}_{\ell,p,\ell-s,\ell-s,s}|_{p=0}
.
In particular, we have, for
n\leq 2
, the expression:

Q_{n}=\frac{(\ell-s)!}{(\ell+s)!}(-1)^{\ell+1}\frac{(2-n)!(\ell+n-2)!}{n!(\ell+2-n)!}.

Finally, it is easy to see that the remaining difference
\alpha^{[s]}_{\mathcal{O}(M^{2})}=\alpha^{[s]}-\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}-\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}
, as a solution to (
14.5
) with the previous
\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}
and
\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}
as inhomogeneities and with trivial data, only produces correction terms at higher order. This concludes the proof of the proposition …∎

…and thus the proof of Thm.
13.1
.
∎

13.3
Treatment of the data of Definitions
8.3
and
8.4

In the above, we discussed the asymptotics arising from data as in Def.
8.2
. We now briefly cover the cases of the other definitions:

Theorem 13.2
.

Let
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
be the unique smooth scattering solution to (
Teuk
) arising from seed data as in Def.
8.3
, i.e. corresponding to parabolic orbits.
Then we have, for any
\ell\geq 2
,

\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{-1/3}(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{4}(r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell})=\frac{(\ell+2)!}{(\ell-2)!}\cdot(-1)^{\ell+1}\sqrt{3}\pi{\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\mathrm{par},\ell}}(\ell+\tfrac{5}{3})(\ell-\tfrac{2}{3})

(13.19)

as well as

\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}=S_{\ell,\tfrac{2}{3},\ell+2,0,-2}\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\mathrm{par},\ell}r_{0}^{-\frac{8}{3}}+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-\frac{8}{3}-\delta}+Mr_{0}^{-\frac{11}{3}}),

(13.20)

with
S_{\ell,\tfrac{2}{3},\ell+2,0,-2}
being defined in (
10.64
).

Remarkably, the decay rate of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
is thus slower in the case of parabolic orbits (cf.
13.10
).

In the case of definition
8.4
, Theorem
13.1
applies with
\underline{\mathscr{B}}=0
. There is something interesting to observe, however. Clearly, we have the following statement if
M=0
:

Theorem 13.3
.

Let
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
be the unique smooth scattering solution to (
Teuk
) arising from seed data as in Def.
8.3
, i.e. corresponding a compactly supported gravitational perturbation along
\mathcal{I}^{-}
.
Moreover, let
M=0
.

Then we have, for any
N\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}

\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}=\left(\sum_{p=2}^{N}\mathscr{E}_{p}S_{\ell,p,\ell+2,0,-2}r_{0}^{-2-p}\right)+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-3-N})

(13.21)

for some generically nonvanishing constants
\mathscr{E}_{p}
.

However, we recall from (
10.65
) that
S_{\ell,p,\ell+2,0,-2}=0
for
\ell\geq p
. In other words, this means that if
M=0
then the limits of
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}
along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
decay like
r_{0}^{-3-\ell}
as
u\to-\infty
.
This is a property that is crucially changed if
M\neq 0
, cf. (
13.10
).

14
Post-Minkowskian expansions of the Teukolsky equation

This section presents a Post-Minkowskian expansion of (
Teuk:s
). The purpose of this is for us to reduce certain somewhat tedious computations to simply solving Minkowskian Teukolsky equations with inhomogeneities.
The results of this section have already been used and referenced in the previous sections for the proofs of Prop.
10.8
, Prop.
11.3
and Prop.
13.2
, i.e. to compute the Minkowskian parts of the solution.
We have also used them to prove (
13.7
).

14.1
The expansion

Recall (
Teuk:s
). We re-write it in coordinates
(r_{0},r,\theta,\varphi)
, denoting
\partial_{u}r_{0}=-(1-\frac{2M}{r_{0}})=-D_{0}
. Since
\partial_{u}=-D_{0}\partial_{r_{0}}-D\partial_{r}
and
\partial_{v}=D\partial_{r}
, we have

-(D_{0}\not{\nabla}_{r_{0}}+D\not{\nabla}_{r})(r^{-2s}D^{s+1}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]})=\frac{D^{s+1}}{r^{2+2s}}\mathring{\not{\Delta}}_{[s]}\alpha^{[s]}-\frac{2MD^{s+1}}{r^{3+2s}}(1+s)(1+2s)\alpha^{[s]}.

(14.1)

Note that

-D\not{\nabla}_{r}(r^{-2s}D^{s+1}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]})=(2sr^{-2s-1}D^{s+2}-(s+1)2Mr^{-2s-2}D^{s+1})\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}-D^{s+2}r^{-2s}\not{\nabla}_{r}^{2}\alpha^{[s]}.

Dividing by
D^{s+1}r^{-2s}
thus casts (
14.1
) into the form

-(\not{\nabla}_{r_{0}}+\not{\nabla}_{r})\alpha^{[s]}+\frac{D_{0}-D}{D_{0}}\not{\nabla}_{r}^{2}\alpha^{[s]}+2s\frac{D}{D_{0}}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}-(s+1)\frac{2M}{r^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}\\
=\frac{\mathring{\not{\Delta}}_{[s]}\alpha^{[s]}}{D_{0}r^{2}}-\frac{2M}{D_{0}r^{3}}(1+s)(1+2s)\alpha^{[s]}.

(14.2)

We now decompose the equation above into the following three equations: Firstly:

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-(\not{\nabla}_{r_{0}}+\not{\nabla}_{r})(r^{-2s}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0})=\frac{\mathring{\not{\Delta}}_{[s]}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}}{r^{2+2s}}},

(14.3)

the Minkowskian Teukolsky equation. Secondly:

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}-(\not{\nabla}_{r_{0}}+\not{\nabla}_{r})(r^{-2s}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}})}\\
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+r^{-2s}\left(\frac{2M}{r_{0}}+(s+1)\frac{2M}{r}\right)\not{\nabla}_{r_{0}}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}}\\
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+r^{-2s}\left((s+2)\frac{2M}{r}\right)\not{\nabla}_{r}^{2}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}-\left(2sr^{-2s-1}(s+2)\frac{2M}{r}+(s+1)2Mr^{-2s-2}\right)\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}}\\
={\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}\frac{\mathring{\not{\Delta}}_{[s]}\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}}{r^{2+2s}}}\\
-{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\frac{2M(s+1)\mathring{\not{\Delta}}_{[s]}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}}{r^{3+2s}}-\frac{2M}{r^{3+2s}}(1+s)(1+2s)\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}},

(14.4)

the Minkowskian Teukolsky equation with inhomogeneity,
and thirdly:

-(D_{0}\not{\nabla}_{r_{0}}+D\not{\nabla}_{r})(r^{-2s}D^{s+1}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{\mathcal{O}(M^{2})})\\
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}-r^{-2s}(D_{0}D^{s+1}-1)\not{\nabla}_{r_{0}}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}-r^{-2s}(D^{s+2}-1)\not{\nabla}_{r}^{2}\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}+2sr^{-2s-1}(D^{s+2}-1)\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}-(s+1)2Mr^{-2s-2}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}-r^{-2s}(D_{0}D^{s+1}-(1-\tfrac{2M}{r_{0}}-(s+1)\tfrac{2M}{r}))\not{\nabla}_{r_{0}}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}-r^{-2s}(D^{s+2}-(1-(s+2)\tfrac{2M}{r})\not{\nabla}_{r}^{2}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}}\\
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+2sr^{-2s-1}(D^{s+2}-(1-(s+2)\tfrac{2M}{r}))\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}-(s+1)2Mr^{-2s-2}(D^{s+1}-1)\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}}\\
=\frac{D^{s+1}}{r^{2+2s}}\mathring{\not{\Delta}}_{[s]}\alpha^{[s]}_{\mathcal{O}(M^{2})}-\frac{2MD^{s+1}}{r^{3+2s}}(1+s)(1+2s)\alpha^{[s]}_{\mathcal{O}(M^{2})}\\
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}+\frac{(D^{s+1}-1)\mathring{\not{\Delta}}_{[s]}\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}}{r^{2+2s}}-\frac{2MD^{s+1}}{r^{3+2s}}(1+s)(1+2s)\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+\frac{(D^{s+1}-(1-(s+1)\tfrac{2M}{r}))\mathring{\not{\Delta}}_{[s]}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}}{r^{3+2s}}-\frac{2M(D^{s+1}-1)}{r^{3+2s}}(1+s)(1+2s)\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}},

(14.5)

the Schwarzschildean Teukolsky equation with inhomogeneity.
Note that adding (
14.3
)–(
14.5
) gives back (
14.1
) with
\alpha^{[s]}=\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}+\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}+\alpha^{[s]}_{\mathcal{O}(M^{2})}
.
The decomposition
\alpha^{[s]}=\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}+\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}+\alpha^{[s]}_{\mathcal{O}(M^{2})}
corresponds to a Post-Minkowskian decomposition in
(r_{0},r)
-coordinates, with an exact keeping track of the error term
\alpha^{[s]}_{\mathcal{O}(M^{2})}
.
In applications, equation (
14.3
)—the Teukolsky equation in Minkowski—is solved first, equation (
14.4
) then becomes a Minkowskian Teukolsky equation with inhomogeneity depending on the already solved for
\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}
, and (
14.5
) is a Schwarzschildean Teukolsky equation with inhomogeneity depending on
\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}
and
\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}
.

We observe that by eq. (
14.3
), the equation (
14.4
) can be rewritten as

{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}-(\not{\nabla}_{r_{0}}+\not{\nabla}_{r})(r^{-2s}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}})=\frac{\mathring{\not{\Delta}}_{[s]}\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}}{r^{2}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+\frac{2M\mathring{\not{\Delta}}_{[s]}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}}{r^{2}r_{0}}-\frac{2M(1+s)(1+2s)\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}}{r^{3}}}\\
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}+(s+1)\frac{2M}{r^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}+\frac{2s}{r}(D_{0}-D)\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}-(D_{0}-D)\not{\nabla}_{r}^{2}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}}.

(14.6)

14.2
Explicitly solving the Minkowskian Teukolsky equation

We now present explicit solutions, supported on fixed angular frequency, of (
14.3
). The following proposition was used in the proof of Propositions
10.8
and
13.2
:

Proposition 14.1
.

Suppose that
\mathscr{A}_{\ell}
is supported on angular frequency
\ell\geq|s|
, that
\ell\geq p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}
, and that

\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}=\mathscr{A}_{\ell}r_{0}^{s-p}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s}S_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}

(14.7)

solves (
14.3
). Then

S_{n}=S_{\ell-s}\frac{(-1)^{\ell-s}(\ell-s)!(\ell-p)!}{(2\ell)!}\cdot(-1)^{n}\frac{(\ell+s+n)!}{n!(s-p+n)!(\ell-s-n)!},

(14.8)

and (
14.7
) indeed solves (
14.3
).
The result holds true for any
p\in\mathbb{R}
if we replace the factor
\frac{(\ell-p)!}{(s-p+n)!(\ell-s-n)!}
by
\binom{\ell-p}{s-p+n}
and extend the binomial coefficient to non-integer arguments in the usual fashion.
Moreover,

(r^{2}\not{\nabla}_{r})^{\ell-s}\alpha^{[s]}=(-1)^{\ell-s}\mathscr{A}_{\ell}r_{0}^{\ell-p}(\ell-s)!S_{\ell-s}.

(14.9)

Proof.

The proof is strictly simpler than the proof of the next proposition.
∎

The following proposition was used in the proof of Propositions
11.3
and
13.2
.

Proposition 14.2
.

Suppose that
\mathscr{B}_{\ell}
is supported on angular frequency
\ell\geq|s|
, that
\ell\geq p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}
, and that

\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}=\mathscr{B}_{\ell}r_{0}^{s-p}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s}S_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\log r_{0}+\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s}R_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\right)

(14.10)

solves (
14.3
). Then

S_{n}=S_{\ell-s}\frac{(-1)^{\ell-s}(\ell-s)!(\ell-p)!}{(2\ell)!}\cdot(-1)^{n}\frac{(\ell+s+n)!}{n!(s-p+n)!(\ell-s-n)!},

(14.11)

and, for
n\leq p-s-1
,

R_{n}=S_{\ell-s}(-1)^{\ell-p+1}\frac{(\ell-s)!(\ell-p)!}{(2\ell)!}\frac{(p-n-1-s)!(n+\ell+s)!}{n!(\ell-s-n)!},

(14.12)

whereas for
p-s\leq n\leq\ell-s
:

R_{n}=S_{n}\frac{R_{\ell-s}}{S_{\ell-s}}+S_{n}\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-s-1-n}\frac{1}{\ell-p-i}.

(14.13)

Moreover, (
14.10
) indeed solves (
14.3
), and

(r^{2}\not{\nabla}_{r})^{\ell-s}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}=(-1)^{\ell-s}\mathscr{B}_{\ell}r_{0}^{\ell-p}\left((\ell-s)!S_{\ell-s}\log r_{0}+(\ell-s)!R_{\ell-s}\right).

(14.14)

Proof.

We compute

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}_{\ell}r_{0}^{s-p}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s}\frac{-n}{r}\left(S_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\log r_{0}+R_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\right),

\displaystyle-\not{\nabla}_{r}^{2}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{B}_{\ell}r_{0}^{s-p}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s}\frac{-n(n+1)}{r^{2}}\left(S_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\log r_{0}+R_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\right),

and

\displaystyle-\not{\nabla}_{r_{0}}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}

\displaystyle=r_{0}^{s-p}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\left((n+s+p)n\frac{1}{r_{0}r}(S_{n}\log r_{0}+R_{n})+\frac{n}{r_{0}r}S_{n}\right)

\displaystyle=r_{0}^{s-p}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s-1}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\left((n+1+s+p)n\frac{1}{r^{2}}(S_{n+1}\log r_{0}+R_{n+1})+\frac{n+1}{r^{2}}S_{n+1}\right).

Inserting the above expressions into (
14.3
) and equating coefficients, we obtain the system of equations (using
n(n+2s+1)-(\ell-s)(\ell+s+1)=(n-(\ell-s))(n+\ell+s+1))
:

\displaystyle(n-(\ell-s))(n+\ell+s+1)S_{n}

\displaystyle=S_{n+1}(n+1)(n+1+s-p),

(14.15)

\displaystyle(n-(\ell-s))(n+\ell+s+1)R_{n}

\displaystyle=R_{n+1}(n+1)(n+1+s-p)+(n+1)S_{n+1}.

(14.16)

The first of these inductively implies (
14.11
).

For the second equation (
14.16
), we first set
n=p-s-1
:

R_{p-s-1}=\frac{p-s}{(p+\ell)(p-\ell-1)}S_{p-s}.

(14.17)

For
n<p-s-1
, we then simply have
(\ell-s-n)(n+\ell+s+1)R_{n}=R_{n+1}(n+1)(p-s-1-n)
, which is solved by

\displaystyle R_{n}

\displaystyle=R_{p-s-1}\frac{(p-s-1)!(\ell-p+1)!}{(\ell+p-1)!}\frac{(p-n-1-s)!(n+\ell+s)!}{n!(\ell-s-n)!}

(14.18)

\displaystyle=-S_{p-s}\frac{(p-s)!(\ell-p)!}{(\ell+p)!}\frac{(p-n-1-s)!(n+\ell+s)!}{n!(\ell-s-n)!}

\displaystyle=S_{\ell-s}(-1)^{\ell-p+1}\frac{(\ell-s)!(\ell-p)!}{(2\ell)!}\frac{(p-n-1-s)!(n+\ell+s)!}{n!(\ell-s-n)!}.

To compute the remaining coefficients, we observe that (
14.16
) implies that
R_{n}=0
for
n>\ell-s
. Finally, for
p-s\leq n\leq\ell-s
, we write
R_{n}=S_{n}\cdot P_{n}
in (
14.12
), which gives:

\displaystyle S_{n}P_{n}=S_{n}P_{n+1}+\frac{1}{n+1+s-p}S_{n},

which is solved by

P_{n-j}=P_{n+1}+\sum_{i=0}^{j}\frac{1}{s-p+n+1-i}

or, equivalently,

P_{n}=P_{\ell-s}+\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-s-1-n}\frac{1}{\ell-p-i}.

(14.19)

∎

14.3
Explicitly solving the inhomogeneous Minkowskian Teukolsky equation

We now prove two statements about eq. (
14.6
). The relevance of the first statement will be to compute the inhomogeneity of (
14.6
), provided that
\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}
is as in Proposition
14.1
.
The purpose of the second proposition will be to provide a simple expression for solutions of (
14.6
), provided that the inhomogeneity is as computed in the first proposition.
The result of the second proposition has already been used in the proof of (
13.7
) and (
13.10
).

Proposition 14.3
.

Define the following functional, denoting the inhomogeneity of (
14.6
):

g[\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}]:=\frac{2M\mathring{\not{\Delta}}_{[s]}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}}{r^{2}r_{0}}-\frac{2M(1+s)(1+2s)\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}}{r^{3}}\\
+(s+1)\frac{2M}{r^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}+\frac{2s}{r}(D_{0}-D)\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}-(D_{0}-D)\not{\nabla}_{r}^{2}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}.

(14.20)

Then, for
\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}
as in Proposition
14.1
, we have that

g[\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}]\cdot\frac{r^{2}r_{0}^{1+p-s}}{2M}=\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s+1}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\underbrace{\left((n-\ell+s)(n+\ell+s+1)S_{n}-(n+s)(n+2s)S_{n-1}\right)}_{:=T_{n}}.

(14.21)

Proof.

By definition of
g
, we have that

\displaystyle g[\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}]\cdot\frac{r^{2}r_{0}^{1+p-s}}{2M}=-(\ell-s)(\ell+s+1)\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}-\frac{r_{0}}{r}(1+s)(1+2s)\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}

\displaystyle+(3s+1)\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}\cdot r_{0}-2s\not{\nabla}_{r}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}\cdot r+(r^{2}-rr_{0})\not{\nabla}_{r}^{2}\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}.

We now insert the specific form of
\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}
and repeat the calculations done in the proof of the previous proposition to get

\displaystyle g[\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}]\cdot\frac{r^{2}r_{0}^{1+p-s}}{2M}=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s}S_{n}[-(\ell-s)(\ell+s+1)]\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}-(1+s)(1+2s)S_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n+1}

\displaystyle-\frac{n}{r}S_{n}[(3s+1)r_{0}-2sr]\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}+n(n+1)\left(1-\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)S_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s}S_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}[-(\ell-s)(\ell+s+1)+2sn+n(n+1)]

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{\ell-s+1}S_{n-1}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}[-(1+s)(1+2s)-(n-1)(3s+1)-(n-1)n].

The result follows from
-(\ell-s)(\ell+s+1)+2sn+n(n+1)=(n-\ell+s)(n+\ell+s+1)
and
-(1+s)(1+2s)-(n-1)(3s+1)-(n-1)n=-(n+s)(n+2s)
.
∎

Proposition 14.4
.

Let
\ell\geq p\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 0}
, and let
\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}
be as in Proposition
14.1
. Then, if
\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}
is of the form

\alpha^{[s]}_{M^{1}}=2Mr_{0}^{s-p-1}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s+1}S^{\prime}_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\log r_{0}/r+Q_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\right)

(14.22)

and solves (
14.6
), we have

S^{\prime}_{n}=S_{\ell-s}\frac{(-1)^{\ell-s}(\ell-s)!(\ell-p)!}{2(2\ell)!}\cdot\frac{(-1)^{n}(\ell+s+n)!}{n!(s-p+n-1)!(\ell-s-n)!}=\frac{s-p+n}{2}S_{n}

(14.23)

and, if
n\leq p-s
:

Q_{n}=S_{\ell-s}(-1)^{\ell-p+1}\frac{(\ell-s)!(\ell-p)!}{2(2\ell)!}\frac{(p-n-s)!(n+\ell+s)!}{n!(\ell-s-n)!},

(14.24)

so
Q_{n}=-\frac{s-p+n}{2}R_{n}
if
n\leq p-s-1
. For
\ell-s\geq n>p-s
, the coefficients
Q_{n}
can all be explicitly expressed in terms of a free constant, say,
Q_{\ell-s}
. Finally,
Q_{\ell-s+1}=\frac{\ell+s+1}{2}S_{\ell-s}
.
With these expressions, (
14.22
) indeed solves (
14.6
).

Proof.

Inserting the ansatz into equation (
14.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\log r_{0}/r[(-n(n+1)-2sn)S^{\prime}_{n}+(n+1)(n+s-p)S^{\prime}_{n-1}]

\displaystyle+\sum\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}[(-2n-1-2s)S^{\prime}_{n}+((n+1)+(n+s-p))S^{\prime}_{n-1}

\displaystyle+\sum\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}[(-n(n+1)-2sn)Q_{n}+(n+1)(n+s-p)Q_{n-1}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(\ell-s)(\ell+s+1)\left(\sum S^{\prime}_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\log r_{0}/r+Q_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}\right)+g[\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}]\cdot\frac{r^{2}r_{0}^{1+p-s}}{2M},

Let us, for now, just write

\displaystyle g[\alpha^{[s]}_{M=0}]\cdot\frac{r^{2}r_{0}^{1+p-s}}{2M}=\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-s+1}T_{n}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}.

(Recall that
T_{n}=0
if
n<p-s
from (
14.21
).)
Then we get the following system of equations for the coefficients
S^{\prime}_{n}
and
Q_{n}
:

\displaystyle(n-(\ell-s))(n+\ell+s+1)S^{\prime}_{n}=

\displaystyle S^{\prime}_{n+1}(n+1)(n+s-p),

(14.25)

\displaystyle(n-(\ell-s))(n+\ell+s+1)Q_{n}=

\displaystyle Q_{n+1}(n+1)(n+s-p)

\displaystyle-(2n+2s+1)S^{\prime}_{n}+(2n+1+s-p)S^{\prime}_{n+1}-T_{n}

(14.26)

We can immediately solve the first equation to obtain (
14.23
).
For the second equation (
14.26
), we observe that, setting
n=\ell-s
,

Q_{\ell-s+1}(\ell-s+1)(\ell-p)=(2\ell+1)S^{\prime}_{\ell-s}+T_{\ell-s},

(14.27)

and, setting
n=\ell-s+1
,

(2\ell+2)Q_{\ell-s+1}=Q_{\ell-s+2}(\ell-s+2)(\ell-p+1)-T_{\ell-s+1}.

(14.28)

In order to ensure that
Q_{\ell-s+2}=0
(and thus
Q_{\ell-s+i}=0
for all
i\geq 2
), we demand that

Q_{\ell-s+1}=\frac{(2\ell+1)S^{\prime}_{\ell-s}}{(\ell-s+1)(\ell-p)}+\frac{T_{\ell-s}}{(\ell-s+1)(\ell-p)}=\frac{-T_{\ell-s+1}}{2\ell+2},

(14.29)

which is a condition on
S^{\prime}_{\ell-s}
:

\displaystyle S^{\prime}_{\ell-s}

\displaystyle=-\frac{(\ell-s+1)(\ell-p)}{(2\ell+1)(2\ell+2)}T_{\ell-s+1}-\frac{T_{\ell-s}}{2\ell+1}

(14.30)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{(\ell-s+1)(\ell-p)(\ell+1)(\ell+s+1)}{(2\ell+1)(2\ell+2)}-\frac{(\ell-s)(\ell+s)(\ell-p)\ell}{2\ell(2\ell+1)}\right)S_{\ell-s}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\ell-p)S_{\ell-s}}{2(2\ell+1)}((\ell-s+1)(\ell+s+1)-(\ell-s)(\ell+s))=\frac{\ell-p}{2}S_{\ell-s}

Here, we used that
T_{\ell-s}=-\ell(\ell+s)S_{\ell-s-1}=(\ell-s)\ell(\ell+s)(\ell-p)S_{\ell-s}/(2\ell)
and
T_{\ell-s+1}=-(\ell+1)(\ell+s+1)S_{\ell-s}
.

We can now freely specify a value for
Q_{\ell-s}
and then solve (
14.26
) to obtain all
Q_{n}
for
p-s<n\leq\ell-s
. Since we won’t need their specific values in this paper, we won’t compute them.

Next, assuming that
p-s\geq 0
(otherwise we’d be done), we insert
n=p-s
into (
14.26
):

(p-\ell)(p+\ell+1)Q_{p-s}=(p-s+1)S^{\prime}_{p-s+1}-T_{p-s},

(14.31)

so
Q_{p-s}
can be directly determined: Since
S^{\prime}_{p-s+1}=S_{p-s+1}/2
, and since

T_{p-s}=(n-\ell-s)(n+\ell+s+1)S_{n}|_{n=p-s}=S_{n+1}(n+1)(n+1+s-p)|_{n=p-s}=S_{p-s+1}(p-s+1),

we get

Q_{p-s}=\frac{p-s+1}{2(\ell-p)(\ell+p+1)}S_{p-s+1}.

(14.32)

Pleasantly, we also have for any
n<p-s
that

(n-(\ell-s))(n+\ell+s+1)Q_{n}=Q_{n+1}(n+1)(n+s-p),

(14.33)

so we can inductively determine all
Q_{n}
for
n\leq p-s
:

\displaystyle Q_{n}

\displaystyle=Q_{p-s}\frac{(p-s)!(\ell-p)!}{(\ell+p)!}\frac{(p-n-s)!(n+\ell+s)!}{n!(\ell-s-n)!}

(14.34)

\displaystyle=S_{p-s+1}\frac{(p-s+1)!(\ell-p-1)!}{2(\ell+p+1)!}\frac{(p-n-s)!(n+\ell+s)!}{n!(\ell-s-n)!}

\displaystyle=S_{\ell-s}(-1)^{\ell-p+1}\frac{(\ell-s)!(\ell-p)!}{2(2\ell)!}\frac{(p-n-s)!(n+\ell+s)!}{n!(\ell-s-n)!}.

This concludes the proof.
∎

15
Remarks on summing up the angular modes

As we have already said in Remark
10.3
, the estimates obtained in this part of the paper cannot directly be summed in
\ell
. For instance, if we write, say,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}=\sum_{\ell=2}^{\infty}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{\ell}
, and insert the estimates for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{\ell}
that we have obtained in Theorem
11.1
, then the
\ell
-dependent constants hiding in the
\mathcal{O}(\dots)
-terms of Theorem
11.1
will not be summable in
\ell
.
To give a concrete example: We cannot directly infer from (
11.5
) of Thm.
11.1
that

\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{4}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}=\sum_{\ell=2}^{\infty}(-1)^{\ell+1}\frac{2M\mathscr{A}_{\ell}}{(\ell-1)(\ell+2)}.

(15.1)

The full resolution of this problem is left to upcoming work
[
KK24
]
, but we here already give an idea how to approach the problem, slightly expanding on the strategy that we have already briefly mentioned in §5(e) of
[
Keh24
]
.

Taking
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
to be as in §
11
, we first write the initial data along
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
(
11.1
) as

\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}={\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}}_{,\mathrm{phg}}+{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}}_{,{\Delta}},

(15.2)

where

{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}}_{,\mathrm{phg}}=\mathscr{A}+\mathscr{B}\frac{\log r_{0}}{r_{0}}+\frac{\mathscr{C}}{r_{0}}.

(15.3)

We now define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M=0,\mathrm{phg}}
as the scattering solution to the
Minkowskian
Teukolsky equation (
14.3
) with scattering data
{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}}_{,\mathrm{phg}}
and no incoming radiation.

From
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M=0,\mathrm{phg}}
, we then define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M^{1},\mathrm{phg}}
as the scattering solution to the
inhomogeneous Minkowskian
Teukolsky equation (
14.4
) with trivial scattering data and with the inhomogeneity given by
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M=0,\mathrm{phg}}
. (To be precise, the inhomogeneity is given by
g[r^{5}\Omega^{-2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M=0,\mathrm{phg}}]
, with
g
defined in Prop.
14.3
.)

Lastly, we define
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{\Delta}
as the scattering solution to the
inhomogeneous Schwarzschildean
Teukolsky equation (
14.5
), with scattering data
{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}}_{{\Delta}}
and with the inhomogeneity sourced by
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M=0,\mathrm{phg}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M^{1},\mathrm{phg}}
.
We note that
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M=0,\mathrm{phg}}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M^{1},\mathrm{phg}}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{\Delta}
.

Of course, in order for these definitions to be well-defined, one briefly needs to extend out scattering theory to allow for suitably decaying inhomogeneous terms.

The point is now that one can use the estimate (
10.15
), the only estimate of this part of the paper where we have sufficiently strong control in
\ell
in order to admit summation in
\ell
, to prove the following statements:

•

First, a slightly more refined version of estimate (
10.15
) directly implies:
|(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M=0,\mathrm{phg}})_{\ell}|\leq C_{\ell}r^{-3}
.

•

Inserting this estimate into (
14.4
) and proceeding as in the proof of (
10.15
), we can then show:
|(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M^{1},\mathrm{phg}})_{\ell}|\leq C_{\ell}r^{-4}
.

•

Inserting both of the above estimates, we can similarly show that
|(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{\Delta})_{\ell}|\leq C_{\ell}r^{-4-\delta}
.

In each of the above estimates, the constant
C_{\ell}
is some constant changing from line to line which, importantly, can be bounded against
\ell^{N}
for an
\ell
-independent integer
N
.

The upshot is that the issue of proving, say, (
15.1
) now entirely reduces to proving robust estimates for the quantity
(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M=0,\mathrm{phg}})_{\ell}
, which can, for instance, be done by proving that
(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M=0,\mathrm{phg}})_{\ell}
satisfies certain persistence of polyhomogeneity results, cf. the brief discussion in §5(e) of
[
Keh24
]
, or, alternatively, by proving ODE estimates for (
14.3
) etc. (It should be possible to prove that
(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}_{M=0,\mathrm{phg}})_{\ell}
is bounded by
C_{\ell}|u|^{2}r^{-5}
using the expressions from §
14
. Such a bound would suffice to prove (
15.1
).)

In order to prove more refined asymptotic statements, e.g. that

\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{5}\log^{-2}r\left(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}-r^{-4}\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{4}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}\right)=\sum_{\ell=2}^{\infty}(-1)^{\ell+1}\frac{3M\mathscr{B}_{\ell}}{\ell(\ell+1)},

(15.4)

one can follow a similar pattern: However, at least if one wants to follow the approach depicted above, one now needs make a stronger assumption on initial data, namely that they admit an expansion up to an error term
{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upalpha}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}}_{,\Delta}=\mathcal{O}(r^{-5})
, and one needs to consider higher-order expansions of the Teukolsky equation (up to order
\mathcal{O}(M^{3})
) as well.

Part III:

Asymptotic analysis of the remainder of the system

In the first two parts of the paper, we have, in particular, defined a class of scattering data describing the far-field region of a system of
N
infalling masses following approximately hyperbolic Keplerian orbits (Definition
8.2
), and we have then found the asymptotic properties of the gauge invariant quantities
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
corresponding to the arising solutions in Theorems
11.1
,
12.1
and
13.1
, respectively.
We recall that the constants in those theorems are related to those of Def.
8.2
and Prop.
8.2
via (
11.2
), (
12.2
) and (
13.5
), which we recall here for convenience:

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}=\frac{(\ell-2)!}{(\ell+2)!}\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell},

\displaystyle\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}=\frac{(\ell-2)!}{(\ell+2)!}\overline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}

(15.5)

We will now use these results to deduce the asymptotic properties of the remaining quantities of the system of linearised gravity.

The case of parabolic orbits (Def.
8.3
) will be left as a simple exercise to the reader; the case of compactly supported gravitational perturbations is included in the discussion of hyperbolic orbits (by setting
\mathscr{A}=0
).

16
Asymptotics for the out- and ingoing null shears
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
and
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{\hat{\underline{\chi}}}}

We begin with the asymptotics of the null shears:
Asymptotic expressions for the in- and outgoing null shears follow by straight-forwardly integrating the transport equations (
3.17b
) and (
3.18b
) from
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
and
\mathcal{I}^{-}
, respectively.

16.1
Asymptotics for
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}

Proposition 16.1
.

Let
\ell\geq 2
. Then the following asymptotic expressions for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
and its
\not{\nabla}_{u}
-derivative are valid in all of
\mathcal{D}
:

\displaystyle\frac{{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}}_{\ell}}{\Omega}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}-\frac{(-1)^{\ell}2M\mathscr{A}_{\ell}}{(\ell-1)(\ell+2)}\frac{1}{r}-\frac{(-1)^{\ell}3M\mathscr{B}_{\ell}}{2\ell(\ell+1)}\frac{\log^{2}r}{r^{2}}+\mathcal{O}\left(M\frac{r_{0}\log r}{r^{2}}+\frac{r_{0}^{2}}{r^{2}}\right),

(16.1)

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{u}(\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}}{\Omega})=\not{\nabla}_{u}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}-\frac{(-1)^{\ell}2M\mathscr{A}_{\ell}}{(\ell-1)(\ell+2)}\frac{1}{r^{2}}-\frac{(-1)^{\ell}3M\mathscr{B}_{\ell}}{\ell(\ell+1)}\frac{\log^{2}r}{r^{3}}+\mathcal{O}\left(M\frac{r_{0}\log r}{r^{3}}+\frac{r_{0}}{r^{2}}\right),

(16.2)

where the limits
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}^{\mathrm{E}}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}^{\mathrm{H}}
in the above are given by:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}^{\mathrm{E}}:=\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}^{E}

\displaystyle=(-\ell(\ell+1)+1)(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})^{\mathrm{E}}_{\ell}-\sqrt{2}\sum_{m=-\ell}^{\ell}Y_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{E},2}(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\uprho}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})_{\ell,m}(-1+(-1)^{\ell}+\ell(\ell+1))

(16.3)

\displaystyle-6(\ell+2)(\ell-1)\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{E}}\cdot\frac{\log r_{0}}{r_{0}}+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-1}),

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}^{\mathrm{H}}:=\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}^{H}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell}(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})^{\mathrm{H}}_{\ell}+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-1}).

In particular, we infer the following

Corollary 16.1
.

For any
\ell\geq 2
, we have

\lim_{v\to\infty}\lim_{u\to-\infty}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}^{\mathrm{H}}_{\ell}=(-1)^{\ell}\lim_{u\to-\infty}\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}^{\mathrm{H}}_{\ell}.

(16.4)

This is the antipodal matching condition, cf. comments below Thm.
1.2
in the introduction.

Proof.

By (
3.17b
), we have

\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}=\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}}{\Omega}-\int_{v_{1}}^{v}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}.

(16.5)

Using the asymptotic estimate (
11.3
) for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}}
, we compute the limit of
r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}
at
\mathcal{I}^{+}
:

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\left.{\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}}{\Omega}}\right|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}-\int_{v_{1}}^{\infty}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}\frac{r_{0}^{n}}{r^{n+3}}\left(\mathscr{A}_{\ell}r_{0}^{2}S_{\ell,0,\ell-2,n,2}+\mathscr{B}_{\ell}r_{0}\log r_{0}S_{\ell,1,\ell-2,n,2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v\right)+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-1})

(16.6)

\displaystyle=\left.{\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}}{\Omega}}\right|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}-(-1)^{\ell}\frac{2(\ell-2)!}{(\ell+2)!}\mathscr{A}_{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n+2}\frac{(\ell+2+n)!}{n!(n+2)!(\ell-2-n)!}

\displaystyle-(-1)^{\ell}\frac{6(\ell-2)!(\ell-1)!}{(\ell+2)!(\ell+1)!}\mathscr{B}_{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n+2}\frac{(\ell+2+n)!}{n!(n+1)!(\ell-2-n)!}+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-1}).

In the second equality above, we inserted the expressions (
10.64
).

We now compute the sums in (
16.6
) using the following two identities:

Lemma 16.1
.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{\geq 2}
. Then:

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n+2}\frac{(\ell+2+n)!}{n!(n+2)!(\ell-2-n)!}

\displaystyle=-1+(-1)^{\ell}+\ell(\ell+1),

(16.7)

\displaystyle(-1)^{\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!(n+2)!}\frac{(\ell+2+n)!}{(\ell-2-n)!}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\ell+2)!}{(\ell-2)!}.

(16.8)

Proof.

The first equation is seen most easily from a relation to
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
(namely the second of (
17.6
) and (
17.7
), cf. Rem.
17.1
), so we’ll here only give the proof of the second equation. We employ the notation that for any finite series
P(x)=\sum_{n=-N}^{N}a_{n}x^{n}
,
[P(x)]_{m}=a_{m}
denotes the
m
-th coefficient of
P(x)
. Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\ell+1)(\ell+2)}\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}\frac{(-1)^{n}}{n!(n+2)!}\frac{(\ell+2+n)!}{(\ell-2-n)!}=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}(-1)^{n}\binom{\ell+2+n}{n}\binom{\ell}{n+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}\left[x^{-n-2}(1-x)^{\ell+2+n}\right]_{-2}\binom{\ell}{n+2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\ell-2}\left[(1-x)^{\ell}\binom{\ell}{n+2}\left(\frac{1-x}{x}\right)^{n+2}\right]_{-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[(1-x)^{\ell}\left(\left(1+\frac{1-x}{x}\right)^{\ell}-1-\ell\frac{1-x}{x}\right)\right]_{-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[(1-x)^{\ell}x^{-\ell}\right]_{-2}=\binom{\ell}{\ell-2}.

∎

Next, we compute the initial data term
\left.{\Omega^{-1}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}}\right|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}
in (
16.6
):
Recall that, by (
8.27
):

\displaystyle\left.{\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}}{\Omega}}\right|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}

\displaystyle=((-2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}-1)\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})_{\ell}-6\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}r_{0}^{-1}\log r_{0}+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-1})

(16.9)

\displaystyle=(1-\ell(\ell+1))\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{\ell,{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}}-3(\ell-1)(\ell+2)\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}r_{0}^{-1}\log r_{0}+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-1}).

Now, on the one hand, we have, by (
8.23
):

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}=\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\uprho}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}},-\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upsigma}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})=\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\uprho}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}},-\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})

(16.10)

\displaystyle\implies\mathscr{A}_{\ell}=\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}((\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\uprho}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})_{\ell},0)-\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\ell+2)!}{(\ell-2)!}(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})^{\mathrm{H}}_{\ell}.

On the other hand, we have (cf. (
11.2
))

\mathscr{B}=\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{1}\overline{\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{1}}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}\underline{\mathscr{B}}\implies\mathscr{B}_{\ell}=\frac{(\ell+2)!}{(\ell-2)!}(\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{E}}-\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{H}}).

(16.11)

Putting all the above equalities together allows us to infer the expressions (
16.3
).

Finally, we can now integrate (
3.17b
) from
\mathcal{I}^{+}
(inserting again (
11.3
)) to compute the next terms in the expansion of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
towards
\mathcal{I}^{+}
, this gives (
16.1
).

The expression (
16.2
) follows in an analogous fashion, using that (
11.3
) commutes with
\not{\nabla}_{u}
.
∎

16.2
Asymptotics for
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{\hat{\underline{\chi}}}}

Proposition 16.2
.

For any
\ell\geq 2
, the following asymptotic expressions for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
and its
v
-derivatives are valid throughout all of
\mathcal{D}
:

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}:=\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}=-\int_{-\infty}^{u}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upalpha}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}=(-1)^{\ell+1}r_{0}^{-2}\left(\frac{6\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}}{\ell(\ell+1)}-2M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\right)+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-2-\delta}+Mr_{0}^{-3})

(16.12)

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{2}\not{\nabla}_{v}}{\Omega^{2}}(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell})=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{\ell,{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}}+\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{n=i+2}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,n,-2}\right)(i-1)!+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-1}\log r_{0}).

(16.13)

Moreover, we have

\left(\frac{r^{2}\not{\nabla}_{v}}{\Omega^{2}}\right)^{2}(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell})=(-1)^{\ell}\frac{(\ell+2)!}{(\ell-2)!}2M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\cdot\log r+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}),

(16.14)

and thus, by the fundamental theorem of calculus:

r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}=\lim_{v\to\infty}r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}-\frac{1}{r}\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{2}\not{\nabla}_{v}}{\Omega^{2}}(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell})+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\log r}{r^{2}}\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{1}{\log r}\left(\frac{r^{2}\not{\nabla}_{v}}{\Omega^{2}}\right)^{2}(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell})+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}r_{0}).

(16.15)

For certain applications, it is crucial to better resolve the structure near
\mathcal{I}^{-}
, so we also record:

\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}}{\Omega}=(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})_{\ell}-\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{n=i+2}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,n,-2}\right)(i-1)!\left(\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{i}-1\right)\\
-r\int_{-\infty}^{u}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upalpha}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\mathcal{O}(\frac{\log r_{0}}{r_{0}}).

(16.16)

Proof.

We will, throughout the proof, make use of the fact that

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,n,-2}=\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\frac{n!}{(n+2)!}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell,n,-2}=0,

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\frac{(n-1)!}{(n+2)!}\underline{S}_{\ell,1,\ell,n,-2}=-3(-1)^{\ell}(\ell-1)(\ell+2),

(16.17)

which is proved in the same way as (
16.8
). (The first identity above can also be seen by restricting (
13.6
) to
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
(where
\frac{r_{0}}{r}=1
)).

We start by using (
3.18a
) to write:

\not{\nabla}_{v}^{n}(\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}}{\Omega})=\not{\nabla}_{v}^{n}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{\ell,{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}}-\int_{-\infty}^{u}\not{\nabla}_{v}^{n}(r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

(16.18)

Next, we compute, using (
13.6
) and (
B.2
)

\displaystyle-\not{\nabla}_{v}(\Omega^{-1}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell})=\int_{-\infty}^{u}\not{\nabla}_{v}(r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

(16.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{u}-\frac{\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}}{r_{0}^{2}}\sum_{n=2}^{\ell+2}(n-1)\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,n,-2}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{n}+(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upalpha}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}})+\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log r_{0}}{r^{3}}+\frac{1}{r_{0}r^{2}}\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\sum_{n=2}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,n,-2}\frac{(n-2)!(n-1)}{(n-1)!}\frac{1}{r}\left(1+\sum_{i=1}^{n-2}\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{i}i!\right)+\int_{-\infty}^{u}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upalpha}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}\log r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}}{r}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{n=i+2}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,n,-2}\right)i!\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{i}+\int_{-\infty}^{u}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upalpha}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\mathcal{O}(r^{-2}\log r),

where, in the last step, we have used that
\sum_{n=2}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,n,-2}=0
(cf. (
16.17
)).
We integrate this in
v
from
{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}
to obtain an expression for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
:

\displaystyle\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}}{\Omega}=\left.{\frac{r^{2}{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}}_{\ell}}{{\Omega}}}\right|_{{\underline{\mathcal{C}}}}+\int\not{\nabla}_{v}(\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}}{\Omega})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

(16.20)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{\ell,{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}}-\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{n=i+2}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,n,-2}\right)(i-1)!\left(\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)^{i}-1\right)-r\int_{-\infty}^{u}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upalpha}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\mathcal{O}(\frac{\log r_{0}}{r_{0}}).

This proves (
16.16
) and, in particular, also implies (
16.12
).
We can hence compute

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v}(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell})

\displaystyle=\lim_{v\to\infty}\left(r\not{\nabla}_{v}(\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}}{\Omega})-(1-\frac{4M}{r})\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell}}{\Omega}\right)

(16.21)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{\ell,{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}}+\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{n=i+2}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,n,-2}\right)(i-1)!+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-1}\log r_{0}).

It is left to prove (
16.14
). For this, we will only compute (and prove the existence of) the limit
\lim\frac{r^{2}}{\log r}\not{\nabla}_{v}(\Omega^{-2}r^{2}\not{\nabla}_{v})(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell})
; it is then left to the reader to check that the remainder term is bounded by
r_{0}
.
We write:

\displaystyle\not{\nabla}_{v}(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v}(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}))

\displaystyle=r\not{\nabla}_{v}^{2}(\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega})+\frac{2M}{r}\not{\nabla}_{v}(\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega})-\frac{4M\Omega^{2}}{r^{2}}\frac{r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}}{\Omega}

(16.22)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-r\left(\int\not{\nabla}_{v}^{2}(r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u\right)-\frac{2M}{r}\left(\int\not{\nabla}_{v}(r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u\right)+\frac{4M}{r^{2}}\left(\int r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u-\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-r\left(\int\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{3}}(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{2}(r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}})-\frac{2M}{r^{3}}(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})(r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\alpha}})+\frac{2M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{2M}{r}\left(\int\frac{1}{r}(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})(r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}})+\left(1-\frac{2M}{\Omega^{2}r}\right)r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{4M}{r^{2}}\left(\int\frac{r}{\Omega^{2}}r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u-\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}}\right)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

A simple integration by parts gives that (we use (
13.6
) in the second line below and only integrate by parts in the third line):

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{2}}{\log r}\left(-r\int_{-\infty}^{u}\frac{2M}{r^{2}\Omega^{2}}r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}-\frac{2M}{r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\frac{4M}{r^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{u}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{2}}{\log r}\left(-r\int_{-\infty}^{u}\frac{2M}{r^{2}}\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}-\frac{2M}{r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\frac{4M}{r^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{u}r\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{2}}{\log r}\left(-\frac{2M}{r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}-\frac{2M}{r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}+\frac{4M}{r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}_{\ell}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\right)=0,

even though each of the individual terms inside the brackets only decays like
1/r
towards
\mathcal{I}^{+}
.

Since the terms with only one
\not{\nabla}_{v}
-derivative in (
16.22
) only contribute at order
r^{-2}
, we can thus infer that

\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{2}}{\log r}\not{\nabla}_{v}\left(\frac{r^{2}}{\Omega^{2}}\not{\nabla}_{v}\right)(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell})=-\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{3}}{\log r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{3}}(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{2}(r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

(16.23)

The limit on the RHS has 3 potential contributions:

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{3}}{\log r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{3}}(\frac{r^{2}}{D}\not{\nabla}_{v})^{2}(r\Omega^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

(16.24)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{3}}{\log r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{3}}\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell,n,-2}+\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}\underline{S}_{\ell,1,\ell,n,-2}\frac{\log r_{0}}{r_{0}})\frac{r_{0}^{n}}{r^{n}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{3}}{\log r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}r^{-3}r_{0}^{-3}\sum_{n=2}^{\ell+3}n(n-1)(\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}\underline{R}^{\prime}_{\ell,n}+2M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\underline{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell,n})\frac{r_{0}^{n}}{r^{n-2}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{3}}{\log r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}\frac{1}{r^{3}}(-1)^{\ell}M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\frac{2\ell(\ell+1)(\ell+2)!}{(\ell-2)!}\frac{\log r-\log r_{0}}{r}.

Let’s compute each line on the RHS of (
16.24
) separately. For the first one, we have:

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{3}}{\log r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}\frac{\Omega^{2}}{r^{3}}\sum_{n=1}^{\ell+2}(\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell,n,-2}+\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}\underline{S}_{\ell,1,\ell,n,-2}\frac{\log r_{0}}{r_{0}})\frac{r_{0}^{n}}{r^{n}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}

(16.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{3}}{\log r}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{\ell}\frac{n!}{(n+2)!}\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell,n,-2}\frac{1}{r^{2}}+\frac{(n-1)!\cdot 2}{(n+2)!}\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}\underline{S}_{\ell,1,\ell,n,-2}\frac{\log r}{r^{3}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-6(-1)^{\ell}(\ell+2)(\ell-1)\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell},

where we have used the integral formulae (
B.1
) and (
B.3
) in the first equality, and then also (
16.17
).

Similarly, we have for the third line of the RHS of (
16.24
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{3}}{\log r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}\frac{1}{r^{3}}(-1)^{\ell}M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\frac{2\ell(\ell+1)(\ell+2)!}{(\ell-2)!}\frac{\log r-\log r_{0}}{r}=0

(16.26)

as a consequence of
\int_{-\infty}^{u}\frac{\log r-\log r_{0}}{r^{4}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}=\mathcal{O}(r^{-3})
, which follows from (
B.3
).

Finally, for the second line of the RHS of (
16.24
), we have

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{3}}{\log r}\int_{-\infty}^{u}r^{-3}r_{0}^{-1}2(\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}\underline{R}^{\prime}_{\ell,2}+2M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\underline{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell,2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}=2(\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}\underline{R}^{\prime}_{\ell,2}+2M\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\underline{Q}^{\prime}_{\ell,2}).

(16.27)

The result (
16.14
) then follows from (
16.27
), (
16.25
) and (
16.23
) by plugging in the expressions (
13.7
) for
\underline{Q}_{\ell,2}^{\prime}
and
\underline{R}^{\prime}_{\ell,2}
. In particular, since
R^{\prime}_{\ell,2}=(-1)^{\ell}3(\ell-1)(\ell+2)
, there is no dependence on
\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}
in (
16.14
).
∎

Remark 16.1
.

Here, we have explicitly computed that the Minkowskian contribution to the
\log
-term in (
16.14
) vanishes. One can also show this indirectly without having to do computations, see also Remark
17.2
.

17
Remaining asymptotics, Bondi normalisation and the laws of gravitational radiation

17.1
Asymptotic behaviour for the curvature components
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{\beta}}
,
\underline{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{\beta}}}
,
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{\rho}}
and
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{\sigma}}

Proposition 17.1
.

For any
\ell\geq 2
, the following asymptotic expressions are valid throughout
\mathcal{D}
:

\displaystyle r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\upbeta}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}+\mathcal{O}(1/r,)

(17.1)

\displaystyle(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}_{\ell},r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}_{\ell})

\displaystyle=(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upsigma}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\uprho}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}})+\mathcal{O}(\frac{r_{0}+M\log r}{r}),

(17.2)

\displaystyle r^{4}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{\ell}2M}{(\ell-1)(\ell+2)}\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\mathscr{A}_{\ell}\log r+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}),

(17.3)

where the limits above are given by

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\upbeta}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}:

\displaystyle=\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\beta}}_{\ell}=-\int_{-\infty}^{u}\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upalpha}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(17.4)

\displaystyle(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\uprho}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upsigma}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}):

\displaystyle=\lim_{v\to\infty}(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}_{\ell},r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}_{\ell})=(-1)^{\ell}(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upsigma}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\uprho}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})_{\ell}-\int_{-\infty}^{u}(\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\upbeta}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}},\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\upbeta}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}.

(17.5)

In particular, we have the relations

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\underline{\upbeta}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}=\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}},

\displaystyle\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upsigma}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}=\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,\mathring{\not{\mathrm{div}}}\,\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}

(17.6)

Proof.

Equation (
17.1
) follows from (
3.27
) and the asymptotic results (
13.6
), (
16.15
) for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\alpha}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
, respectively.
The expression for the limit then follows from (
3.28a
).

Similarly, equation (
17.2
) follows from applying Theorem
12.1
to
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
via (
3.38
) in the case of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}
, whereas the result for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}
follows from considering
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\Psi}}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\Psi}}
together with the asymptotics for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
from Propositions
16.1
and
16.2
.
The expressions for the limits then follow from (
3.30a
), (
3.29a
) together with the fact that
\lim_{v\to\infty}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\left(\Omega\mathrm{tr}\underline{\chi}\right)}_{\ell}=0
, which we will independently show in Prop.
17.2
, and lastly using that

\lim_{u\to-\infty}\lim_{v\to\infty}(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}_{\ell},r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}_{\ell})=(-1)^{\ell}\lim_{u\to-\infty}\lim_{v\to\infty}(r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\rho}_{\ell},r^{3}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}_{\ell})=(-1)^{\ell}(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\uprho}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}},\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upsigma}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})_{\ell},

(17.7)

which follows from (
12.5
) and (
8.30
).

Finally, the expression (
17.3
) for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
follows from integrating (
3.31b
).
∎

Remark 17.1
.

Notice that (
17.7
), combined with (
17.6
), implies the identity (
16.7
).

17.2
Asymptotic behaviour for the connection coefficients
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{\eta}}
,
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{\underline{\eta}}}
,
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{\omega}}
and
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mbox{\scalebox{0.4}{(1)}}}}{{\Omega}}

We can deduce immediately from the relation (
3.18b
) and the asymptotic expressions for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
from Propositions
16.1
and
16.2
the following:

Corollary 17.1
.

For any
\ell\geq 2
, we have that
17
17

17

Recall that e.g.
(\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\eta}})_{\ell}^{\mathrm{E}}=\sum_{m=-\ell}^{\ell}(\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\eta}})_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{E}}\cdot Y_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{E},2}=\sum_{m=-\ell}^{\ell}\sqrt{\tfrac{1}{2}(\ell-1)(\ell+2)}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\eta}}^{\mathrm{E}}_{\ell,m}\cdot Y_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{E},2}
.

r^{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\eta}}_{\ell}=\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upeta}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}/r),

(17.8)

where

-2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\upeta}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}:=\lim_{v\to\infty}-2r^{2}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\eta}}_{\ell}=\lim_{v\to\infty}(r^{2}\not{\nabla}_{v}(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell})-r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell})\\
=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{\ell,{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}}+\underline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\left(\sum_{n=i+2}^{\ell+2}\underline{S}_{\ell,0,\ell+2,n,-2}\right)(i-1)!-(-\ell(\ell+1)+1)(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})^{\mathrm{E}}_{\ell}\\
-\sqrt{2}\sum_{m=-\ell}^{\ell}Y_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{E},2}(\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\uprho}_{{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}})_{\ell,m}(-1+(-1)^{\ell}+\ell(\ell+1))-(-1)^{\ell}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{\ell,{\mathcal{S}_{\infty}}}^{\mathrm{H}}+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-1}\log r_{0}).

(17.9)

Similarly, by combining (
3.18b
) with (
3.20b
), we obtain

Corollary 17.2
.

For any
\ell\geq 2
, we have

4\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\omega}_{\ell}=(-1)^{\ell+1}2M(\overline{\mathscr{A}}_{\ell}+\mathscr{A}_{\ell})\frac{\log r}{r^{3}}+\mathcal{O}(\frac{r_{0}}{r^{3}}).

(17.10)

Proof.

In view of the asymptotics for
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\eta}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}
,
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}
and
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\chi}}}
found so far, we deduce from (
3.18b
) and (
3.20b
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{2r^{3}}{\log r}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\omega}_{\ell}

\displaystyle=\lim_{v\to\infty}\frac{r^{2}}{\log r}\not{\nabla}_{v}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}(r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\eta}}_{\ell})-\frac{r^{4}}{\log r}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}_{\ell}

(17.11)

\displaystyle=\lim_{v\to\infty}-\frac{1}{2}\frac{r^{2}}{\log r}\not{\nabla}_{v}\frac{r^{2}\not{\nabla}_{v}}{\Omega^{2}}(r\Omega\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\underline{\hat{\chi}}}_{\ell})-\frac{r^{4}}{\log r}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\beta}}_{\ell}.

The result then follows by inserting (
17.3
) and (
16.14
).
∎

Remark 17.2
.

Recall Remark
16.1
: If there were a
\underline{\mathscr{B}}
-dependent Minkowskian contribution to the limit, then this would appear in the expression for
\lim\frac{r^{3}}{\log r}\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\omega}_{\ell}
as well. One could then deduce a contradiction from the fact that
\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\mathring{\not{\nabla}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\omega}_{\ell}
is only supported on electric angular modes.

Next, we use (
3.17a
) to deduce

Corollary 17.3
.

For any
\ell\geq 2
, we have that

r\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\eta}}_{\ell}=\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upeta}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}+\mathcal{O}(1/r),

(17.12)

where

-2\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upeta}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}:=\lim_{v\to\infty}-2r\mathring{\not{\mathcal{D}}}^{\ast}_{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\eta}}_{\ell}=\not{\nabla}_{u}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\upchi}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}+\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\hat{\underline{\upchi}}}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}=-6(\ell+2)(\ell-1)\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell}^{\mathrm{E}}\cdot\frac{\log r_{0}}{r_{0}^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-2}).

(17.13)

Remark 17.3
.

In fact, the limit (
17.13
) is supported entirely on electric angular modes. This can be seen, for instance, from (
3.24
), the fact that magnetic angular modes are not in the kernel of
\mathring{\not{\mathrm{curl}}}\,
, and
\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\sigma}}=0
.

Finally, we have:

Corollary 17.4
.

For any
\ell\geq 2
and any
m\in\{-\ell,\dots,\ell\}
, we have
\lim_{v\to\infty}{\mathring{\not{\nabla}}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}}_{\ell}=\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\upeta}}_{\ell,\mathcal{I}^{+}}
and thus:

\lim_{v\to\infty}\Omega_{\ell,m}=-3\sqrt{2\frac{(\ell-1)(\ell+2)}{\ell(\ell+1)}}\underline{\mathscr{B}}_{\ell,m}^{\mathrm{E}}\cdot\frac{\log r_{0}}{r_{0}^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(r_{0}^{-2}).

(17.14)

Proof.

This follows from (
3.12
) and Corollaries
17.1
and
17.3
.
∎

17.3
Future Bondi normalisation of the solution

Corollary
17.4
shows that the solution has a nonvanishing limit of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}
along
\mathcal{I}^{+}
, that is, the Bondi normalisation of the solution towards the past (cf. Def.
5.3
) does not imply Bondi normalisation towards the future, which we define as follows:

Definition 17.1
.

A solution to (
3.9
)–(
3.32
) is called Bondi normalised towards the future/
\mathcal{I}^{+}
if

\lim_{v\to\infty}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}=\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\left(\Omega\mathrm{tr}\underline{\chi}\right)}=0=\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{K}=\lim_{v\to\infty}{\mathrm{tr}{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{g}}}}=\lim_{v\to\infty}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\hat{\not{g}}}.

(17.15)

(Notice that the vanishing of the limits of
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\left(\Omega\mathrm{tr}\underline{\chi}\right)}
and
{\mathrm{tr}{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{g}}}}
is automatically enforced by the vanishing of the other three limits.)

Remark 17.4
.

It may at first not be entirely clear that this definition is just the future version of Def.
5.3
; this is because in the latter, we demand the vanishing of
\lim_{u\to-\infty}r^{-1}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{b}
along
\mathcal{I}^{-}
and the vanishing of
\lim_{u\to-\infty}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\hat{\not{g}}}
only for
v=1
. In essence, however, this is equivalent to demanding that
\lim_{u\to-\infty}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\hat{\not{g}}}
vanish along all of
\mathcal{I}^{-}
.

Corollary
17.4
shows that our solution is not Bondi normalised towards the future, although we will see below that the last three quantities of (
17.15
) do, in fact, vanish (as a consequence of our solution being Bondi normalised towards the past).

We can remedy the failure of the solution to be Bondi-normalised towards the future by adding an ingoing gauge solution that kills of
\lim_{v\to\infty}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}
and
\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\left(\Omega\mathrm{tr}\underline{\chi}\right)}
while simultaneously decaying sufficiently fast towards
\mathcal{I}^{-}
to leave the Bondi normalisation near
\mathcal{I}^{-}
intact.

Definition 17.2
.

Define the ingoing gauge function
\underline{f}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}(u,\theta,\varphi)
via:

\underline{f}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}_{\ell,m}(u)=\int_{-\infty}^{u}\lim_{v\to\infty}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}_{\ell,m}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u^{\prime},

(17.16)

and define
\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{f}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}
to be the ingoing gauge solution induced by
\underline{f}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
as in Def.
4.4
.

Finally, define
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathrm{Bondi}}:=\mathfrak{S}-\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{f}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}
, where
\mathfrak{S}
is the solution of Proposition
8.2
.

All statements made henceforth will concern the solution
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathrm{Bondi}}
.

Notice that the subtraction of
\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{f}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}
modifies equations (
8.24
)–(
8.29
) at next-to-leading order.

Concerning this new difference solution, we then have the following statement:

Proposition 17.2
.

The results of Propositions
16.1
–
17.1
and Corollaries
16.1
–
17.2
still apply to
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathrm{Bondi}}
; i.e. all changes induced by subtraction of
\mathfrak{S}_{\underline{f}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}}
are subleading.
In addition,
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathrm{Bondi}}
satisfies for any
\ell\geq 2

\lim_{v\to\infty}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}_{\ell}=\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\eta}}_{\ell}=\lim_{v\to\infty}r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\left(\Omega\mathrm{tr}\underline{\chi}\right)}_{\ell}=0=\lim_{v\to\infty}r^{2}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{K}_{\ell}=\lim_{v\to\infty}{\mathrm{tr}{\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\not{g}}}}_{\ell}=\lim_{v\to\infty}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\hat{\not{g}}}_{\ell}.

(17.17)

Finally,
\mathfrak{S}_{\mathrm{Bondi}}
obeys, for any
\ell\geq 2
:

\lim_{v\to\infty}\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{b}_{\ell}=0.

(17.18)

Proof.

The fact that Propositions
16.1
–
17.1
and Corollaries
16.1
–
17.2
remain unchanged follows directly from Prop.
4.4
.

Next, the limits of
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}_{\ell}
and
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{{\eta}}_{\ell}
vanish by construction (we have chosen
\underline{f}^{\mathcal{I}^{+}}
to kill off
\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\Omega}
, after all.)

To see that the limit of
r\accentset{\scalebox{.6}{\mbox{\tiny(1)}}}{\left(\Omega\mathrm{tr}\underline{\chi}\right)}_{\ell}
